# Progress Tracker — Form-Builder SaaS

**Single source of truth for "where are we right now."** Keep the top three sections
(Current Status · Next Session · the G table) current. Full per-session narrative history
lives in [PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md](PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md) and in git — do **not** re-read it to resume.

## Standing Rules

1. **Keep this tracker current** — update "Current Status" + "Next Session" as work completes. Never leave the repo in a state the next session has to reverse-engineer.
2. **One shared design system, no exceptions** — every page (builder, dashboard, inbox, settings, public runtime) uses the same app shell + component library + tokens (plan Main Feature #6 / DSR doc #19). Any exception needs a documented rationale.
3. **Fully styled by default** (confirmed 2026-07-05) — every page ships fully styled from shared components; only pure backend/API increments have no styling work. Don't re-ask "styled vs unstyled?" per increment.
4. **End every session** (confirmed 2026-07-10, **reaffirmed a third time 2026-08-08**) with (a) a save to the MD files changed this session — this tracker's top sections + one dated line in `PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md`, plus any `docs/**`/ADR/plan actually touched — and (b) a copy-paste "what to prompt next" line. Keep it lean: update the top of this file + append one archive line; don't rewrite the whole history. **The closing chat message is a 3–5 bullet status, then the fenced next-prompt ALONE** (amendment 2026-08-08): the bullets are a scannable header, and the ban from 2026-07-23/07-31 still holds for everything in and around the fenced block — no env caveats, gate results, decisions or lessons in chat, they live here. Never hand off leaving the next session to reverse-engineer state from git.

5. **Build everything buildable — the user's testing NEVER gates development** (confirmed 2026-08-06, and it SUPERSEDES the 2026-08-05 "leave every PR open until the app is testable" instruction). Their words: *"let's focus on all the tasks that can be made by you. I will test the app when it is completely built so it will not delay the development. I don't want any pending tasks to be missed."* So: never defer, park, or leave a PR unmerged because the user cannot verify it yet. Merge into the integration branch on green and keep going. `main` still receives nothing until the ONE final integration PR — that part of the original intent stands. **The only surviving blocker is an input the user alone possesses** (OCR scans; third-party OAuth credentials; a Stripe account; hardware), and even then, build every part that does not need it. A design decision the user has not made is NOT automatically a blocker: propose one, recommend, and proceed unless it is genuinely theirs (pricing, legal, an authorization widening).

   **⛔ AMENDED 2026-08-12 — A HELD ROW IS OUT OF SCOPE, NOT PENDING. STOP SURFACING IT.** The user's words: *"i told you that we will skip all tasks that needs my interaction. so you can focus on all the tasks that you can do by yourself. as long as the task doesnt require the tasks that i told you to put on hold."* Rule 5 already said their testing does not gate building; this closes the other half — **a held row must not be reported, counted, scheduled, or asked about at all.** It stopped being work the moment it was held, and re-enters ONLY on the user's explicit signal.

   **THE HELD LIST IS CLOSED AND NAMED** — nothing joins it without the user saying so: the **OCR chain** (H1d → H18a/b → H19a/b) · **all uploading / import features** incl. bulk Excel/CSV (the 2026-08-09 explicit-signal decision) · **payments** (H1c, H20, Cashier/Stripe-Tax) · **Track B** deployment · **GDPR / legal / pricing** calls.

   **THE FAILURE MODE IS REPORTING, NOT BUILDING** — building held rows had already stopped; surfacing them had not. Four consequences. **(1) Progress percentages and pending-task counts are computed over BUILDABLE scope only** — a held row is not a denominator. State held rows separately and briefly, or omit them; never as "blocked on you", never as the reason a phase is incomplete. The 2026-08-12 status report is the worked example of getting this wrong: it opened Phase 3 with "cannot be finished without you" — true and useless — while burying H16b/H16c, the two rows that *can* be finished. **(2) Never present a held row as the next step, and never re-derive its blocker for the user** — they know; they are the one who held it. **(3) A held row never blocks its neighbours** — build every non-held row in the queue regardless of where the held one sits in the order. **(4) Do not ask which of two buildable lanes to take** — when a second agent is running, take the lane with NO file overlap and proceed. An `AskUserQuestion` about lane choice was rejected on 2026-08-12 for exactly this; an alignment question is warranted only when both lanes touch the same files.

   **⚠️ AND NEVER IDLE ON A HELD ROW — ADVANCE TO THE NEXT PHASE AUTOMATICALLY (user decision 2026-08-12).** Their words: *"in case nothing is left to do in the phase 3 and i still did not ask to start what i told you to hold, will we start phase 4 already? it should be."* So when the current phase's only remaining rows are held, **start the next phase without asking**; a held row never stalls the queue and never keeps a phase "open". **Phase 4 is not wholly buildable either** — payments was cut *into* it from Phase 3 and is held, and GDPR *tooling* is buildable while the GDPR/legal/pricing *call* is not — so enter Phase 4 on **SSO/SAML, dedicated-DB tenancy, data residency and CRDT sync**, and apply this same rule again inside it. ⚠️ Note the queue order this does NOT change: Phase 3's non-held remainder is only **H16b/H16c**, but JR3–JR5, J3, J4, J5 and the final integration PR are all still non-held work sitting ahead of Phase 4.

   **⚠️ WHEN A SECOND AGENT HAS THE WORKING TREE, MAKE NO EDITS TO IT — INCLUDING THIS FILE.** Both sessions share one checkout, so a tracker write lands on whatever branch the other agent has checked out, and a branch switch yanks the tree out from under them mid-run. Use `git worktree add --detach <scratch> origin/phase1-completion`, commit there, and `git push origin HEAD:phase1-completion` — which is how this very amendment was landed. **Say plainly in the hand-off that the tracker was not updated** if it could not be.

   **⛔ AMENDMENT 2026-08-17 — THE NEXT-PROMPT IS OWED WHEN THE WORK IS DONE, NOT WHEN THE USER ASKS A QUESTION. DO NOT PAUSE MID-ROW. APPLIES TO BOTH LANES.** The user's words: *"why did we pause if its not done? … if we are done. please provide the next prompt unless, nothing is left in the pipeline for the specific lanes. this is applicable for both lanes."*

   **A QUESTION IS NOT A STOP INSTRUCTION.** Asked *"are we done here?"* with half a row unbuilt, the correct answer is **"no, X remains"** — and then **keep building.** Handing back a fenced next-prompt at that moment reads as *"this is finished"*, which is the one thing it must never say. That is exactly what happened on 2026-08-17: J4c1 had merged, J4c2 was untouched, and the session closed out as if the row were complete.

   **THREE CONSEQUENCES.** **(a) Only close out when the ROW is genuinely complete** — a split row (J4a/J4b/J4c, J4c1/J4c2, J3a/J3b/J3c) is not done until every half has merged. **(b) When a lane's queue empties, Rule 7(f) governs and it does not say "stop"** — claim the unassigned remainder, and only the lane that empties LAST recommends the final integration PR. **(c) If a pause is genuinely needed** — a background gate, a CI run, a container build — **say so in chat in one line using the word *wait*, per Rule 6**, and then resume without being asked. Silence during a long run is indistinguishable from being finished, which is the failure this amendment and Rule 6 both exist to prevent.

6. **Signal every wait — never end a message ambiguous** (added 2026-08-08, the in-session half of Rule 4's hand-off contract). Before ANY pause — a background agent, the Pest/Vitest/PHPStan gate suites, `npm ci`, a container build, waiting on a CI run — **say so in one line first**: what is running, roughly how long, and that nothing is needed from the user yet. Use the word *wait* explicitly; "let me check…" is not a signal. **Say "done" when it returns**, before the results. **Mark 👉 your turn** when the turn is genuinely theirs. The user's words (2026-08-08): *"everytime you pause, make sure to inform me that I need to wait and you are working on something in the background so I won't be asking if we are waiting for something and ask you if we can move forward to the next session… I must be notified because I cannot read everything you say."* Silence during a long run is indistinguishable from being finished — this rule exists so "am I waiting, or is Claude working?" is never answerable only by guessing. Chat text only; the user chose this over a desktop/sound alert, so there is no hook to maintain.

7. **TWO AGENTS = TWO WORKTREES, ONE DATABASE** (added 2026-08-12, user decision). Running two sessions in parallel is supported and encouraged, but **only** under this protocol. It supersedes Rule 5's "make no edits while another agent has the tree", which was the emergency workaround for having no protocol at all.

   **(a) ONE WORKTREE PER AGENT — NEVER TWO SESSIONS IN ONE CHECKOUT.** A checkout has exactly one HEAD, so a `git checkout` in one session silently relocates or clobbers the other's uncommitted work. Lane A keeps `c:\laragon\www\dev_formbuilder_app`; lane B gets its own directory sharing the same `.git`:
   `git worktree add ../fb-lane-b -b <branch> origin/main`
   Git refuses to check out one branch in two worktrees — that is a guardrail, not an obstacle. Remove a finished lane with `git worktree remove ../fb-lane-b`.

   ✅ **REWRITTEN 2026-08-18 — `main` IS THE TRUNK AND `phase1-completion` IS RETIRED.** PR #179 (`52754ed`) merged the integration branch into `main`, the two trees are identical, and the user's decision the same day is that **new work branches from `origin/main` and PRs into `main`, self-merged on 6/6 green**. `phase1-completion` is no longer checked out in any worktree, so the 2026-08-15 note that it *“is checked out in `fb-lane-b`, so the other lane cannot check it out at all”* is **spent** — it described a lock that no longer exists. ⚠️ **WHAT SURVIVES IT IS THE HALF THAT WAS NEVER ABOUT THAT BRANCH: ALWAYS `git fetch` AND BRANCH FROM `origin/main`, NEVER FROM LOCAL `HEAD`.** Each worktree still sits on whatever it last shipped (Lane A on `j8-tracker`, Lane B on its last increment), so a branch cut from local `HEAD` is cut from a stale base and inherits none of what the other lane has since fixed. That is a property of two worktrees, not of one integration branch, and it outlives the branch that taught it.

   **(b) ASSIGN LANES BY SUBSYSTEM, NOT BY ROW.** Two rows from the same queue usually touch the same files; two *subsystems* do not. Both PR into `phase1-completion`; **never PR one lane into the other.**

   **⚠️ AMENDED 2026-08-15 — THE BOUNDARY LIVES HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE, BECAUSE TWO COPIES OF IT DRIFTED APART AND NEITHER LANE COULD SEE IT.** Each lane's next-prompt had been *restating* its own version of the split, and by 2026-08-15 the two statements **contradicted each other**: Lane A's conceded five named subtrees to Lane B, while Lane B's claimed **all** of `app/Services/`, **all** of `app/Support/` and **all** of `config/`. Lane A's very next row (J4b) edits `app/Support/Navigation/CrumbTrail.php`, which is Lane A's under one map and a trespass under the other — **and both lanes would have been reading their own map correctly.** That is the ADR-0017 collision shape exactly (each lane checked, each was right, the artefact still collided), so the fix is the same one: **one authority, referenced rather than copied.** A next-prompt may now say only *"the boundary is Standing Rule 7(b)"* — **a next-prompt that restates the table is the defect, not a convenience.**

   | LANE B owns | LANE A owns | NEITHER — claim in this file FIRST |
   |---|---|---|
   | `app/Services/{Sso,Tenancy,Connectors}/` | `packages/design-system/` | `docs/` (except each lane's own ADR) |
   | `app/Support/{Sso,Tenancy,Connectors}/` | **all of** `resources/js/**` (widened 2026-08-25) | `PROGRESS.md` — own block only, per (d) |
   | `config/`, `scripts/`, `app/Http/Middleware/` | `app/Support/Navigation/` | `openapi.json`, `ci.yml`, `phpunit.xml` |
   | `app/Http/Controllers/Tenant/Sso/` | controller wiring for Lane A's OWN pages | ADR numbers, migration prefixes — see (g) |
   | `resources/public-runtime/` (CRDT row) | `MEMORY.md` — Lane A only; Lane B never writes memory | `tests/e2e/` — grep it before ANY selector change |

   **⚠️ WIDENED 2026-08-25 — LANE A'S COLUMN IS NOW ALL OF `resources/js/**`, NOT THREE NAMED SUBDIRECTORIES.** The old entry named `{Pages,Layouts,components/shell}/` and left `components/builder/`, `components/integrations/`, `components/notifications/`, `components/submissions/`, `composables/` and `types/` unassigned — every one of which Lane A's own queue opens. Unassigned is not neutral: it is where two lanes each read the map correctly and still collide. The statement now is one line: **Lane A owns `resources/js/**` and `packages/design-system/**`; Lane B owns the PHP application plus `resources/public-runtime/**`.**

   **⛔ (b-bis) PAIRED FILES — A GATE IN ONE LANE'S TREE THAT READS ANOTHER LANE'S TREE OFF DISK (added 2026-08-25).** Three files in this repository assert parity across the lane boundary, so **either lane can turn the other's suite red over a file its own diff does not contain.** They are not a hazard to be routed around — they exist precisely to force both halves of a contract to move together. **THE RULE: a lane that changes one half CLAIMS AND EDITS BOTH HALVES IN THE SAME PR.** Splitting a paired change across two lanes is the one thing that cannot work, because whichever lands first breaks the other.

   | Gate | Reads | Fires when |
   |---|---|---|
   | `packages/design-system/src/theme/__tests__/clipped-node-containment.test.ts` (Lane A's tree) | `SCAN_ROOTS` at `:38` covers `packages/design-system/src`, `resources/js` **and `resources/public-runtime`** | `KNOWN_UNGUARDED` at `:71` still lists `RuntimeShell.vue` — **Lane B's** — and `:162` asserts **exact equality**, so the list moving in either direction reddens it |
   | `tests/Feature/Notifications/NotificationTypeParityTest.php` (Lane B's subject) | `resources/js/components/notifications/types.ts` (Lane A's) | either side adds a `NotificationType` — it asserts the same cases **in the same order** |
   | `tests/Unit/Navigation/ShellAbilityParityTest.php` (Lane B's subject) | `resources/js/components/shell/nav-model.ts` and `resources/js/types/inertia.d.ts` (Lane A's) | either side adds an ability key |
   | `packages/design-system/src/theme/__tests__/token-references.test.ts` (Lane A's tree) | `APP_SCAN_ROOT` at `:163` is `resources/` **entire** — `resources/js`, `resources/public-runtime` and `resources/css` alike | any `var(--mds-<ramp>-<step>)` appears in application code; the ramp set is read from `tokens/primitive.json`, so it widens itself when a ramp is added (added M23, 2026-08-26) |
   | `scripts/component-import-lint.php` (**Lane B's column**, per the table above) | `SCAN_ROOTS` at `:63-67` is all three front-end trees — `resources/js`, `resources/public-runtime` **and** `packages/design-system/src` | any `.vue` file in either lane uses a component in its template without importing it. Registered in `composer.json` **and** as its own `ci.yml` step, so it is merge-blocking |

   ⛔ **THE FOURTH ROW IS A PROHIBITION, NOT A PARITY ASSERTION, AND THE TABLE'S REMEDY DOES NOT APPLY TO IT.** The three rows above it are two lists that must move together, where whichever lane lands first breaks the other — so *"claim and edit both halves in the same PR"* is the fix. This one has no second half: it is green until somebody writes a primitive ramp token, it names the offending file in its own failure message, and it reddens inside the PR of whichever lane wrote it. A reader applying the remedy literally would go looking for a file that does not exist. It is listed here anyway because it is the same *shape* — a gate in Lane A's tree reading Lane B's off disk — and because the sweep that built this table would not have found it either: `APP_SCAN_ROOT` is composed with `join()`, which is exactly the "path from a variable" case the note below says the sweep misses.

   ⚠️ **THE SWEEP THAT FOUND THESE MATCHED LITERAL PATH STRINGS IN `.ts`/`.php` GATE FILES, SO IT IS A FLOOR AND NOT A CENSUS.** A gate composing its path from a variable or a glob would not have matched. **Treat "a red gate naming a file my diff does not touch" as a paired-file symptom, not a flake** — and add it to this table when you find it.

   **THE RULE THAT MAKES THE TABLE HOLD: `app/Services/` AND `app/Support/` ARE NOT WHOLESALE LANE B'S.** Lane B owns the three named subtrees under each; everything else beneath them is unclaimed and taken per-row. `app/Support/Navigation/` is Lane A's on the merits — **Lane A wrote `CrumbTrail` in J2d** when trail resolution moved server-side, and no Lane B row goes near it. A lane whose row needs PHP outside its column does not stop: it claims it per the paragraph below.

   **`resources/public-runtime/` GOES TO LANE B FOR THE CRDT-SYNC ROW** (user decision 2026-08-15) — 19 files (`sw.ts`, `outbox.ts`, `useSyncOutbox.ts`, `SubmissionOutbox.vue`, `SyncStatus.vue` + five test files) that a CRDT rewrite lands in by definition; splitting one offline-sync rewrite across two agents costs more than one agent crossing a line. ⚠️ **THE COST IS STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED: that directory is one of the three chunks Lane A's Vitest gate sums**, so Lane A's **110 files / 1,883 tests** baseline MOVES when CRDT lands. Lane A re-measures after it; it does not assume.

   **(c) ⚠️ THE DATABASE IS THE ONE THING WORKTREES DO NOT DUPLICATE, AND IT IS DESTRUCTIVE.** There is exactly one `dev_formbuilder_app-postgres-1` and one `dev_formbuilder_app-app-1` (verified 2026-08-12). Pest runs `migrate:fresh`, which **DROPS THE SCHEMA** — so two concurrent Pest runs mean one silently wipes the other mid-run, surfacing as bizarre irreproducible failures that read as product bugs. **Gate runs must be serialized.** Before any Pest run, check and wait if it returns non-zero:
   `docker exec dev_formbuilder_app-app-1 sh -c "ps -eo args | grep '[p]est' | wc -l"`
   (Use `wc -l`, not `grep -c` — `grep -c` exits 1 on zero matches and reads as a command failure.) Same single-instance constraint applies to `npm run dev` on port 5173 and to the seeded dev data.

   **✅ AMENDED 2026-08-12 — SERIALIZATION IS RETIRED, BECAUSE THE PREMISE ABOVE IS NO LONGER TRUE. The paragraph is kept because it is right about the DANGER and only wrong about the topology.** "The database is the one thing worktrees do not duplicate" was a statement about how the stack happened to be run, not about worktrees — and a worktree can carry its own compose stack. **Lane B now does**: an UNTRACKED `docker-compose.override.yml` in `c:\laragon\www\fb-lane-b` sets `name: fb-lane-b`, so Compose derives `fb-lane-b-*` containers, an `fb-lane-b_default` network on which the other stack is unreachable by service name, and a separate `fb-lane-b_pgdata` volume. Host ports are remapped app **8081** · postgres **5433** · redis **6380** · vite **5174** · mailpit **1026/8026**; verified free before use, and every published port carries the `!override` tag because **`ports:` merges by APPEND** — without it the second stack inherits 8080/5432/6379/1025/8025/5173 as well and collides on all six.

   **WHAT MAKES THE TEST DATABASE FOLLOW AUTOMATICALLY, AND IT IS ONE LINE THAT WAS ALREADY THERE.** `phpunit.xml` pins `DB_DATABASE=meridian_testing` but says nothing about `DB_HOST`, which falls through to `.env`'s **service name** `postgres`. A service name resolves on the calling container's OWN network, so `meridian_testing` inside `fb-lane-b-app-1` IS `fb-lane-b-postgres-1`'s copy. `migrate:fresh` can only drop the schema of the lane that ran it — **structurally, not by convention**. The same indirection puts the seeded dev data and `npm run dev` (5174, moved INSIDE the container too, or `public/hot` would point the browser at the other lane's Vite) on the same footing.

   **MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED (2026-08-12).** Lane A's dev database read `tenants=2 users=10 forms=7 submissions=518` before and after a full `migrate:fresh --seed` in Lane B — identical — while Lane B's own copy came up independently at `users=9 submissions=534`. `tests/Feature/Connectors` then ran to **153 passed / 593 assertions** entirely inside `fb-lane-b-app-1`. A fresh `pgdata` volume also re-runs `docker/postgres/init/*.sql`, so the three RLS roles, `meridian_testing` and PostGIS arrive for free — **keep that bind mount** or every migration fails on a missing `meridian_app`.

   **THE PROBE STAYS, SCOPED.** Two agents sharing ONE stack is still the destructive case, and that is what the `ps -eo args` check is for — run it against whichever container you are about to test in. What is retired is the requirement to serialize ACROSS lanes. **Both files are untracked and gitignored by name**: `.gitignore:41` ignores `docker-compose.override.yml` and `.gitignore:3` ignores `.env` — and note `.gitignore:3` is the literal `.env`, **not** `.env*`, so `.env.lane-b`, `docker-compose.lane-b.yml` and `compose.lane-b.yml` are all committable and must not be used for this. Two costs, stated rather than discovered: `composer install` on this bind mount exceeds composer's 300 s process timeout (`-e COMPOSER_PROCESS_TIMEOUT=0`), and a second stack means a second `.env`, so a change to one lane's environment does not reach the other.

   **(d) THE TRACKER AND MEMORY NEED A WRITER DISCIPLINE.** Both lanes will want this file. Conflicts are survivable when the regions differ (Standing Rules vs Current Status auto-merged cleanly on 2026-08-12), so **each lane writes only its own Current Status block** and neither reformats the other's. **Memory is more dangerous than the tracker**: `MEMORY.md` and the files beside it are shared, outside git, and **last-write-wins** — two agents editing one memory file loses one silently. Announce a memory write, or leave memory to one lane.

   **(e) THE HAND-OFF IS PER-LANE, NOT PER-SESSION — RULE 4 BREAKS UNDER PARALLELISM AND THIS IS THE FIX.** Rule 4 says "end every session with a save and a copy-paste next-prompt", written when there was exactly one session; with two lanes both would write *the* next prompt and clobber each other. So: **each lane closes with its own 3–5 bullet status and its own fenced next-prompt**, and writes that prompt to **its own labelled line** in `## Next Session — Resume Here` — `**LANE A NEXT PROMPT →** `Read PROGRESS.md, including Standing Rule 7. You are LANE A (design), in the main working tree on phase1-completion. Your subsystem is packages/design-system/, resources/js/Pages/ and resources/js/Layouts/ — do not edit Lane B's (app/Services/Connectors/, app/Support/Connectors/, config/connectors.php, connector pages). Lane A owns MEMORY.md; Lane B does not write memory. **JR5 IS MERGED — PR #142 (`e02a8ce`), 6/6 with real steps; CI Pest 3637/0 (15,468 assertions; the +22 on 3615 is Lane B's H16c, not JR5, which touches no PHP), E2E 506 passed / 0 failed, Storybook axe 223 across 33 suites (unchanged, deliberately), Vitest 101 files. The LAST JR row, so the Vivid re-skin is COMPLETE.** The builder switches panes on `@container (max-width: 60em)` of its own box; `showBuilderPane()` in tests/e2e/support/navigate.ts is the shared way into a builder pane. **BUILD J3 — the auth & registration vertical.** Branch j3-auth-vertical off phase1-completion, PR into it, self-merge on 6/6 green. Scope (PROGRESS.md's J-queue row): `MdsPasswordStrength` live checklist; the four character classes at `FortifyServiceProvider.php:42` alongside min-12 + HIBP; **enforce `verified` — it guards ZERO routes today** while `MustVerifyEmail` and the notice page already exist; a welcome email (none exists); a **"member joined" Owner notification as a new `NotificationType`, NOT a `DomainEventType`** (`JoinTenantOnRegistration` is silent to the Owner today); **Google-only** social login (Socialite, built against a fake per the H15a precedent — live credentials are a user input and do not block the build); the split-panel auth layout; and the four deferred auth-axe pages. ⚠️ **THE FOUR 2026-08-09 USER DECISIONS ARE DECISIONS OF RECORD — do not re-ask** (password policy; Google only; uploading/OCR wait for an explicit signal; gamification last). ⚠️ **READ THE JR5 BLOCK IN CURRENT STATUS FIRST — five of its lessons are about to recur. (a) VERIFY THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE BEFORE PLANNING AGAINST IT — that is now FOUR-FOR-FOUR on finding the row's own framing wrong** (JR5 was described as "three panes → three tabs at ≤480px" and the builder already linearized at ≤1024px; the real defect was a 185px canvas across 1025–1200 that no gate had ever rendered). J3's row names specific file:line claims — check every one. **(b) THE VISUAL SWEEP IS THE ONLY GATE THAT SEES THIS WORK.** inotify does not cross the Windows Docker bind mount, so Vite never sees an SFC edit: `docker restart dev_formbuilder_app-node-1` after every edit, then confirm what Vite actually serves (`curl -s 'http://localhost:5173/@fs/var/www/html/<path>.vue?vue&type=style&index=0&scoped=true&lang.css' | grep <your-declaration>`) BEFORE believing a frame. Drive http://demo.localhost:8080 as owner@demo.test / meridian-demo-2026 with a standalone Playwright script (NOT the e2e config); pass `executablePath: 'C:/Users/DOH/AppData/Local/ms-playwright/chromium-1234/chrome-win64/chrome.exe'`; **emulateMedia reducedMotion BEFORE flipping data-theme-mode**; navigate on `domcontentloaded` + a hydrated selector, never `networkidle`. ⚠️ **The tenant host answers in ~40–70s per request** (`artisan serve` is single-threaded, `PHP_CLI_SERVER_WORKERS` unset) — set Playwright timeouts to 180s and **navigate ONCE then resize**, rather than re-navigating per viewport. **(c) GREP tests/e2e/ FOR ANY SELECTOR YOUR CHANGE COULD INVALIDATE** — `auth-axe.spec.ts` and `responsive-axe.spec.ts` both drive the auth pages, the e2e suite CANNOT run on this host, and this is the J2b 81-failure shape. **(d) MEASURE, DO NOT ESTIMATE.** JR5's plan predicted a ~250px→~140px toolbar; the truth was 369px→225px, and the sweep also turned up a defect the plan never anticipated. **(e) A GATE YOU CANNOT RUN IS NOT A GATE YOU MAY ASSUME** — `token-references.test.ts`, `theme-overrides.test.ts`, `type-scale.test.ts`, `DataTable.test.ts` and now `SegmentedControl.test.ts` all read SOURCE TEXT and can red-light a CSS-only change. ⚠️ **GATES:** Pest as `docker exec dev_formbuilder_app-app-1 php -d memory_limit=2G vendor/bin/pest <dir>` — Lane B has its own stack, no serialization needed; **PHPStan local baseline is 20 — measure the DELTA** (all 20 are `property.notFound` phantoms CI does not report); the three lint gates **on the HOST** at 84 controllers / 93 migrations / 28 jobs (Lane B moves the migration count); `openapi.json` BYTE-IDENTICAL — **J3 touches PHP, so expect this to move and regenerate it deliberately**; vue-tsc on the host as `node node_modules/vue-tsc/bin/vue-tsc.js --noEmit` AND `-p tsconfig.sw.json`. ⚠️⚠️ **THE LOCAL VITEST POOL IS BROKEN ON THIS HOST AND IT FAILS *GREEN*, WHICH IS WORSE THAN THE WARNING THIS LINE USED TO CARRY.** A full `vitest run --no-file-parallelism` took 88 minutes and reported **"50 passed (50)" with 50 `Failed to start forks worker` errors and EXIT CODE 0** — half the suite silently never ran and the file count itself lied. `--pool=threads` starts workers reliably but OOMs on the full set; **chunk by directory** (`packages/design-system`, `resources/public-runtime`, `resources/js`) and sum. **The file count is 101.** ⚠️ **`tsconfig.json` EXCLUDES `resources/js/**/*.test.ts` AND DOES NOT COVER `tests/e2e/`**, so `npm run type-check` never sees either and Playwright is transpile-only — type-check them yourself with `node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --ignoreConfig --noEmit --strict --target es2022 --module esnext --moduleResolution bundler --skipLibCheck --types node <files>`. **⛔ DO NOT REPORT A LOCAL FULL-SUITE PEST NUMBER** — CI is the authority, and compare a branch number only against a run of the same base. On finish: update LANE A NEXT PROMPT only, append your own dated archive entry, then take the next row in the Lane A queue (J4 — the ~15 missing design-system primitives, which is where `MdsTabs` is finally owed; DSR §3.4 carries a note NOT to retrofit it onto the builder).`

   ⚠️ **AND THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE CONTAINS A VERBATIM EXAMPLE OF THE HAND-OFF LINE, WHICH MAKES THIS FILE HOSTILE TO A NAIVE SEARCH-AND-REPLACE — LEARNED BY DOING IT TWICE (2026-08-16).** A script that replaced from the FIRST occurrence of the hand-off marker to the Lane B one deleted **1,086 lines** — the whole of Current Status, the roadmap and the ledger — because the first occurrence is the example a few lines up, not the real line ~1,085 lines below. It merged, because a docs-only change is green by construction and the `grep -c` used to check afterwards returned **1**, which reads as success and is in fact the symptom: there should have been TWO.

   ⚠️ **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS VERY WARNING MADE IT WORSE, WHICH IS WHY THE RULE BELOW IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** It advised anchoring on the line start — and *quoted the marker to say so*, at a point where the quote wrapped onto a fresh line. That created a THIRD line-start occurrence, inside the note warning about the first two, so a script following its advice would have deleted the file again. **NAME THE THING, NEVER QUOTE IT** — fifth occurrence in this project, and the only one so far to booby-trap its own remedy.

   **SO: match the hand-off marker only where it begins a line, assert it appears exactly once that way, and — the part that actually saves you — ASSERT THE RESULTING LINE COUNT IS WITHIN A PLAUSIBLE DELTA.** A marker check tells you what you found; only the line count tells you what you destroyed.

   **THE LANE QUEUE IS THE CLAIM, AND A CLAIM IS A COMMIT.** A lane takes the top unclaimed row from its own queue and needs no permission. To take a row from the *other* lane's queue — or from the unassigned remainder — **write the claim into this file and commit it FIRST**, because the commit is the only thing the other lane can see. Never start an unclaimed row on the strength of "the other lane probably isn't on it."

   **THE TWO QUEUES, so a fresh agent needs no briefing** (boundaries are in (b); these are ROWS only — refreshed 2026-08-15):
   **LANE A (design)** — ~~JR3~~ → ~~JR4~~ → ~~JR5~~ → ~~J3~~ → ~~J4a~~ → ~~J4b1~~ → ~~J4b2~~ → **J4c → J5.** *(2 rows left; J4b shipped as #158 + #162)*
   **LANE B (backend)** — ~~H16b~~ → ~~H16c~~ → ~~P1a/P1b/P1c (SSO/SAML)~~ → ~~P2a~~ → ~~P2b~~ → ~~P2c~~ → ~~P1d~~ → ~~P1e~~ → ~~`P3a` — the cross-device draft concurrency guard (this WAS the "CRDT sync" row, rescoped on the evidence; user decision 2026-08-17)~~ → **THE FULL GAMIFICATION ENGINE (claimed under 7(f) — see the ledger in (g)), decomposed into five: ~~`K1a` points engine (MERGED, PR #169)~~ → ~~`K1b` badges (MERGED, PR #171)~~ → ~~`K1c` streaks + team progress + the `audits` backfill (MERGED, PR #173)~~ → **`K1d` leaderboard + API** → `K1e` the UI.** *(the named queue was EMPTY and 7(f) is in force; ⚠️ **`K1e` runs LAST because it needs `Dashboard.vue`, `DashboardController`, `DashboardMetricsService` and `packages/design-system/`, all of which are Lane A's live J5 claim — J5 HAS NOW MERGED (#170), so those files are released and K1e is unblocked — it still runs LAST in the K-map order*)*
   ⚠️ **CRDT-PROPER IS FORECLOSED, NOT PENDING — THE THIRD ROW OF THAT SHAPE IN THIS QUEUE AND THE LAST ONE ON IT.** Deferred on the record in FIVE documents, and its revisit trigger (*"real usage data shows concurrent multi-device editing is common"*) is an input nobody here can supply — the data-residency shape exactly. **The row also lost its own qualifier in transcription**: `PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md:207` reads "CRDT sync **if needed**". What replaces it is not a substitution but the defect verifying the row uncovered — `docs/offline-first-sync-design.md` §8's premise is **false as built** (H9b/H10 shipped cross-device resume carrying the SAME `client_submission_uuid`), and the draft channel has **no lost-update guard** while its sibling edit channel already does. Full reasoning and the file claim are in (g).
   ⚠️ **P1e IS NEW ON 2026-08-15 AND IT IS A SECURITY ROW, NOT A SPLIT FOR CONVENIENCE.** Verifying P1d's row against the code found the SAML **login** arm carrying no browser-flow binding — structurally the login-CSRF J3c2's adversarial pass found in the Google path (`docs/security-threat-model.md:165`). `SsoLoginController` writes nothing to the session at mint, and the ACS calls `Auth::login()` with no comparison against anything the *starting* browser held; `request_id`/`InResponseTo` prove **this SP minted this flow** and say nothing about **who holds it** — the provenance-read-as-origin error that row already names for ADR-0009 §D3. **The fix is not Google's one-liner**: the ACS is a genuinely cross-site POST under `SameSite=Lax` and structurally cannot read the minting browser's session, so closing it means giving the login arm the same same-site completion hop the **step-up** arm already has and that `:165` already calls "the precedent that DOES bind." User decision 2026-08-15: **reproduce it first, then split** — P1d records it as the document's second `Open` verdict, P1e builds the hop. **CRDT sync is therefore the last named Lane B row after P1e, and Rule 7(f) governs from there.**
   ⛔ **DEDICATED-DB TENANCY AND DATA RESIDENCY ARE FORECLOSED, NOT PENDING — they were in this queue and are now OFF it.** Database-per-tenant is rejected on the record (`docs/adr/0017-tenant-isolation-tiering.md`: identity resolves BEFORE any tenant exists, and three RLS policies subquery a neighbouring table, which PostgreSQL cannot express across databases); the named target is schema-per-tenant in the SAME instance, and P2a/P2b/P2c discharged what is buildable of it. Residency is ADR-0017 §D6: a **hosting** decision, not a schema one, whose revisit trigger ("a second hosting region exists") is an infrastructure input nobody here can supply. **Do not re-derive either; do not build a `tenants` column for either.**

   **(f) WHAT A LANE DOES WHEN ITS QUEUE EMPTIES — AND HOW THE USER FINDS OUT (added 2026-08-15, user request).** Nothing in this file used to say. Both lanes would simply stop, and the user would have to ask.

   **THE ONE UNASSIGNED NON-HELD BUILD ROW IS THE FULL GAMIFICATION ENGINE** (points / badges / streaks / leaderboards — the 2026-08-09 decision of record puts it as *the very last increment before deployment*; J5 ships only the light getting-started checklist). It is in NEITHER queue above, which means **today both lanes would report "queue empty" with a real build row still outstanding**. So: **the FIRST lane to empty claims gamification** — per the claim-is-a-commit paragraph — and does **not** open the integration PR and does **not** idle.

   **THE THREE CHECKPOINTS, NONE OF THEM SILENT** (this is Rule 6 applied to the end of the queue rather than to a pause):
   1. **A lane's last row merges** → it writes `LANE <X> QUEUE EMPTY — <date>` into its own Current Status block **and says so plainly in chat**, naming what it claims next (gamification) or that it is now waiting on the other lane.
   2. **Both queues empty** → the lane that empties LAST announces **both** lanes complete and **RECOMMENDS the final integration PR rather than performing it.** `main` has received nothing since the I-map began, so this is the highest-consequence action in the project and it is the user's to approve.
   3. **The merge lands** → the held list is presented as the go-forward pipeline and the turn is marked **👉 your turn**.

   **THE FINAL INTEGRATION PR → `main` BELONGS TO NEITHER LANE AND IS THE ONE THING THAT MUST WAIT FOR BOTH.** It opens only when **both** queues are empty; whichever lane empties last owns it. A lane that finishes first does **not** open it — it claims from the remainder instead. **Give it its own session**, not a tail-end of the last build row: it wants a real review pass and a green CI run of its own, ✅ **ALL THREE CHECKPOINTS ARE DISCHARGED AS OF 2026-08-18.** Checkpoint 2 ran as written — recommended, approved, then performed — and the merge landed as PR #179 (`52754ed`, 419 commits, 1,074 files, `main` 6/6 green on its own post-merge run). Checkpoint 3 fired the same day: the held list was presented as the go-forward pipeline and **the user's answer is *not yet — ask again later***, which by Rule 5's amendment means it is not surfaced, counted or scheduled again until they signal. ⚠️ **AND THE CLAUSE THIS SENTENCE USED TO CARRY IS SPENT: `ci.yml`'s `workflow_dispatch` + nightly `schedule` TRIGGERS ARE NOW LIVE**, because GitHub reads both from the default branch only and that file has now reached it — inert for the entire I-map, real for the first time today. `gh workflow run` is a control alongside `gh run rerun`. ⚠️ **`deploy.yml` FIRED ON THE MERGE AND WAS SKIPPED**, which is the dormancy proven empirically rather than argued from the YAML: `DEPLOY_ENABLED` and every repo secret are still unset, so **a merge to `main` deploys nothing** — which is what makes the trunk workflow in (a) safe.

   **⚠️ THE HELD LIST RE-ENTERS AT CHECKPOINT 3 AND AT NO OTHER MOMENT.** Rule 5's amendment stands unchanged — a held row is not reported, counted, scheduled or asked about — and the user's 2026-08-15 instruction (*"recommend a merge so all the remaining task that needs me will be next on the pipeline"*) re-opens it **only once the merge has landed**. Recorded here so the hand-off costs zero re-discovery: **OCR chain** (sample filled/handwritten scans + ground truth; the Vision key is already supplied and I12's blank-form print renderer already produces the artefact to fill in) · **all uploading / import** (the explicit go signal, nothing else) · **payments** (a Stripe account) · **Track B deployment** (the ADR-0005 Windows Server host, deliberately not stood up) · **GDPR / legal / pricing** (genuinely the user's calls — the tooling is buildable, the decisions are not). ⚠️ **AND ONE THING THAT IS NOT A HELD ROW BUT BECOMES REAL AT THE SAME MOMENT: live third-party credentials** — Google OAuth (J3c2 shipped against a fake, per the H15a precedent), plus Sheets and Airtable. Those are deployment inputs, not scope.

   **(g) A GLOBALLY-NUMBERED ARTEFACT IS A SHARED-NAMESPACE ALLOCATION AND CANNOT BE CHOSEN SAFELY FROM INSIDE ONE LANE.** Reading the current maximum is **not** a reservation: two lanes read the same maximum and both act on it. That is how `0017` came to mean two different ADRs (P2a's tenant-isolation doc and J3c2's Google doc), costing the per-occurrence sweep in PR #153 — **133 references across 66 files, 72 moved and 61 stayed, decided by reading each line rather than by path.** The same defect produced a duplicate migration prefix (`2026_08_16_000001` twice), harmless only by luck. **Claim the number in THIS FILE and commit it before using it.**

   **⛔ AMENDED 2026-08-25 — A CLAIM IS A *PUSHED* COMMIT, AND CLAIMS NOW LIVE IN `docs/claims/`. AN UNPUSHED CLAIM DOES NOT EXIST.** M14 wrote a correct, complete claim and it was invisible to everyone, because it sat on a branch in `fb-lane-b` that was never pushed — the rule said *commit*, the rule meant *publish*, and for the whole of that increment the other lane had nothing to check against. Two changes follow. **(1) Claims move out of this file into `docs/claims/lane-a.md` and `docs/claims/lane-b.md`, ONE WRITER EACH.** Two lanes appending to one shared block is a merge conflict waiting for the day both lanes claim on the same afternoon; two lanes writing two separate files cannot conflict **structurally**, which is the same reasoning that put each lane's status block and hand-off line in its own region under (d) and (e). **(2) The claim is pushed to `main` BEFORE the first file is opened** — `git pull --rebase && git push origin HEAD:main` on a docs-only commit touching one file no other lane writes. It cannot conflict on content; only on the fast-forward race, which the rebase resolves. **Before opening any shared or paired artefact, `git fetch` and read BOTH files.** A file open in a lane and absent from `docs/claims/` is the defect, not the collision that follows it.

   **`docs/claims/decisions.md` IS THE THIRD FILE, AND IT EXISTS SO THAT NO LANE EVER IDLES ON A QUESTION.** Rule 5 already says a design decision the user has not made is not automatically a blocker; this is where that becomes mechanical. A lane reaching a genuine product call **appends the question, the two or three real options, and its own recommendation — then takes the next row in the same turn.** The user answers in batches. It is not a queue of blockers; it is a queue of *decisions*, and the lane keeps building past it.

The row's other example (an Editor collaborating on one form) is exactly right. **The one it does not name is worse than either**: a **Reviewer** holds `submissions.create` and CAN mint the token, while `SubmissionPolicy::create()` requires `forms.edit.any` or EDITOR capacity since the deliberate G10a tightening and a reviewer's grant is reviewer capacity — so a Reviewer was authorized to encode on **ZERO** forms through the web app and reached **EVERY** form through this route. **Read the ability map and the role matrix before believing any claim about who can exploit an ability gate.**

Six are right to be (`tenant.show`; both `field-library`; `form-templates.index`; `gamification.me`; and `form-templates.store`, which gates in its controller). The eighth was **`GET /api/v1/sync/manifest`**, serving the COMPLETE `schema_snapshot` of any published or superseded version to any holder of `read:forms` while `GET /forms/{form}/versions/{version}` gated the identical payload on `can:view,form`. **Grepping the shape rather than trusting the row's count is now five-for-five** (M8, M9, M11, M12, M13).
     ⛔⛔ **TWO MORE OUTCOMES ESCAPED `replayOne()` AND ABORTED THE WHOLE BATCH, AND THE AUTHORIZATION FIX FORCED A DECISION ON THE FIRST.** (a) `forms` soft-deletes and `form_versions` does not, and **neither RLS policy filters `deleted_at`** (read off `pg_policies`, not assumed) — so a deleted form's versions stay resolvable, the pipeline's own `Form::findOrFail()` raised a `ModelNotFoundException` nothing caught, and the request 404'd **after earlier items had already committed**, re-raising on every retry so one poisoned row **stalled a device's outbox permanently**. There was no way to write the gate without deciding this: it needs a `Form`, so the choice was `withTrashed()` (authorize against a deleted form — wrong) or `findOrFail` (the bug). (b) **`FormNotAcceptingSubmissionException` is a SIBLING of `SubmissionException`, not a subclass** — every exception in that directory is `final class … extends RuntimeException` — so a closed, not-yet-open or **at-capacity** form rendered as a top-level 403 and lost every item's result. **That is the response cap the row itself cites as the consequence, arriving as the one refusal the batch could not survive.** The method's docblock had claimed *"Never throws"* since G8b: an intention read afterwards as a measurement, for the third increment running.
     ⚠️ **MUTATION MATRIX: 7 MUTATIONS, 0 UNDEFENDED, AND THE DECISIVE ONE ADDED A TEST RATHER THAN CHANGING CODE.** M1 (delete the guard) reddens 4. **M2 (gate on `FormPolicy::view` instead of `SubmissionPolicy::create`) reddens EXACTLY ONE** — a synthetic member who may edit the form but holds no `submissions.create` — and that case exists **only because the matrix was planned before the tests were written**: all five seeded roles give the two policies the same answer here, so the wrong policy would have been invisible to the other twenty-six cases. M4 (`withTrashed()`) reddens 1; **M5 (drop the acceptance catch) reddens 2 — closed AND capacity, separately**, which is what proves those two paths are not sharing a test; M6 (hoist the manifest gate above the status filter) reddens 1; M7 (control) survives. ⛔ **M1 AND M3 (throw instead of returning a per-item error) REDDEN THE IDENTICAL SET, AND REORDERING THE MIXED BATCH TO SEPARATE THEM DID NOT WORK.** From a client's side *"an unauthorized item is its own `forbidden` result and the batch continues"* is **one** observable, which both a missing guard and a throwing guard violate; **nothing can observe a guard's absence without observing a refusal.** Recorded as what the contract IS rather than engineered around — the M12 rule says read the matrix, and reading it sometimes ends in a statement instead of a new case. The reorder was kept anyway, because it made the case measure the forward direction (a refusal does not STOP what follows) instead of the backward one no implementation was going to get wrong.
     ⚠️ **ONE CASE WAS VACUOUS AND WAS CAUGHT BY DESIGNING ITS MUTATION FIRST.** *"404s an unknown version id for an unentitled caller"* pins nothing: with no version resolved there is no form to gate on, so **every** ordering answers 404. It was replaced by a **never-published DRAFT** version — the only input that exists, belongs to a real form, and is still withheld — which is the sole shape where the two orderings differ. M6 then reddened it. **A test whose mutation you cannot write is a test that measures nothing.**
     ⚠️ **THE STRUCTURAL GUARD STATES ITS OWN LIMIT.** `tests/Feature/Api/GroupBPolicyGateTest.php` walks the LIVE route table (non-vacuity asserted in both directions: >50 routes walked, no stale allowlist entry, and the gated majority outnumbers the allowlist 4:1), because this defect class is a property of a route TABLE and no controller's test can see the next route somebody adds. **It notices a route that ARRIVES ungated; it cannot judge whether an allowlisted reason is TRUE** — deleting either in-controller gate leaves it green, which the behavioural cases catch instead. Written into the file so nobody reads it as more than it is. ⚠️ Its first draft matched on `Illuminate\Auth\Middleware\Authorize:` because that is what **`route:list` PRINTS**; `gatherMiddleware()` **RETURNS the declared alias** `can:view,form`, so it found ZERO gated routes and reported all fifty as ungated. It now resolves through the router's own alias map. **The command's output is not the API's output.**

     ⚠️⚠️ **VERIFYING THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE CONFIRMED ALL SEVEN OF ITS CLAIMS VERBATIM AND STILL FOUND FIVE THINGS IT DOES NOT NAME — THIRTY-THREE-FOR-THIRTY-THREE.** All five are in the closed backlog row; the two worth carrying forward are **(2)**, because it changes the severity class — the pre-lock `SemanticResult` also feeds `FinalizedStatus::for()`, so a racing save is the difference between `submitted` and `screened_out` and therefore between **consuming a purchased `max_responses` slot and not**, on a row simultaneously labelled *"was shown no questions"* about somebody who answered some — and **(5)**, a sweep of every `lockForUpdate()` in `app/` that found **no other pre-lock-read → whole-document-write site**. `SubmissionPipeline::persist()` creates its row inside the transaction; `PublishService`, `RestoreService`, `FormBuilderService`, `FormService`, `ScopeNodeService` and `SubmissionReviewService` all read after their lock. **`promote()` was the last door, and a clean sweep is a result rather than a non-event** (the M10 precedent).
     ⛔⛔ **AND THE ADVERSARIAL PASS CORRECTED THE ROW'S OWN FRAMING, WHICH IS THE FINDING WORTH CARRYING FORWARD ABOVE THE FIX.** The row motivates the defect with the two-device resume, and **on that flow P3a's baseline check usually fires first**: both DRAFT channels set `checkBaseline: true` unconditionally and say so in writing (`GuestDraftRequest`, `EncodeDraftRequest`), so a second device saving from a stale base is refused before it can move anything. The windows M12 **uniquely** closes are narrower and elsewhere: **`/api/v1/submissions/{submission}/promote`, which runs no `saveDraft()` at all and has therefore never had a first check** — and is the seam the OCR review-and-confirm flow is documented to reuse; a **SUBMIT that sends no baseline**, because the submit requests gate on `claimsBaseline()` rather than unconditionally; **`createDraft()`'s 23505 fold**, which passes `checkBaseline: false` by design; and the genuine sub-window during Stage 3 on any channel. **A row can be true in every clause and still point at the wrong flow — so ask which flow the fix actually covers, not only whether the row is right.**
     ⚠️ **MUTATION PASS: 7 MUTATIONS, ZERO UNDEFENDED, AND THE ONE THAT MATTERED CHANGED THE TESTS RATHER THAN THE CODE.** M1 (delete the guard — the defect reintroduced) reddens **exactly the seven refusal cases** and leaves both controls green. M2 (checksum check before the status re-assert) reddens **exactly one**, the idempotent-no-op case, so the ORDER is pinned. M3 (compare the recomputed hash instead of the stored value — the plausible-wrong token) reddens **two, one of them a PRE-EXISTING test** whose planted row carries a null checksum. M6 (a semantically identical read) survives as a control must. **M4 (loose `!=`) survives BY CONSTRUCTION** — 64-hex-or-null, exactly M11's finding, kept strict because that is a property of the DOMAIN rather than of the operator. ⛔ **M5 (hoist the guard out of the transaction) INITIALLY REDDENED THE SAME SINGLE CASE AS M2, WHICH MEANT *"the comparison reads UNDER THE LOCK"* WAS ENFORCED BY A COMMENT AND NOT BY A TEST** — every existing case stages its write during Stage 3, which a hoisted check still sees. The new case stages it AFTER the lock is granted, and **states what it cannot measure**: its writer takes no `submissions` lock, which no production path does, so it pins a property of the CODE rather than a reachable race; and its write shares promote's own transaction, so the refusal rolls it back and the *"the other device's answers survive"* assertion belongs to the pre-lock cases instead. **A mutation that reddens the same case as another mutation is telling you two different properties share one test.**
     ⚠️ **TWO ASSERTIONS WERE WRONG BEFORE THEY SHIPPED, AND BOTH IN DIRECTIONS THIS PROJECT HAS PAID FOR.** Two channel cases compared the surviving document with `toBe()`, which is **KEY-ORDER sensitive** on arrays while a jsonb round-trip returns the database's order — the identical trap `SubmissionAnswerEditService::sameAnswer()` documents for the audit diff; they ksort first and stay strict on the values. And the lock-placement case asserted a survivor that its own staging destroys. **Ask what your measurement could not have seen** — here, that everything is staged inside RefreshDatabase's single transaction, so no test independently verifies the cross-connection READ COMMITTED premise; that reasoning is inherited from `updateDraft()`'s documented one and is stated rather than proven.

Scoping Stage 2b turned it red, and **the red was the correct signal rather than a regression**: the controller and Stage 2b now share one predicate, so no sequentially-planted row can be visible to one and not the other, and the ONLY remaining way in is the real interleaving.      ⚠️⚠️ **VERIFYING THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE CONFIRMED IT VERBATIM AND STILL FOUND FOUR THINGS IT DOES NOT NAME — TWENTY-NINE-FOR-TWENTY-NINE.** **(1) THE ADOPTION CONSUMES THE REAL INVITEE'S EMAILED LINK.** `TenantMembershipService::attachMember():385` force-fills `invite_token => null`, so after the takeover the legitimate invitee's link 404s at `InvitationController::resolvePendingInvite()` — the attack destroys the victim's own evidence trail and is indistinguishable from an ordinary expired invite. **(2) THE ATTACKER CHOOSES THE PRIVILEGE LEVEL.** `SsoUserProvisioner::roleFor():122-131` lands an `Invited` membership at the **invited** role, not at `default_role_name`, so inviting as `admin` yields an admin session. **(3) TURNING JIT PROVISIONING OFF DOES NOT CLOSE IT** — `:95` exempts `Invited` from the toggle by explicit condition, so there is no configuration a workspace can adopt to protect itself. **(4) AND ONE DEFECT THAT IS NOT THIS ROW AT ALL, FOUND WHILE SCOPING THE VUE FILE AND FILED NOWHERE:** `resources/js/Pages/invitations/Show.vue:49` renders `<MdsBanner>` with **no import**, and `resources/js/app.ts:29-32` registers no components globally (`.use(plugin)` is Inertia's), so the expired/already-used-invitation error banner **renders nothing at all**. Every other consumer in the tree imports it. It is inside this increment's file set, so it is fixed here rather than filed.
     **THREE USER DECISIONS, 2026-08-24 — DO NOT RE-ASK.** **(1)** The refusal asks M8's own predicate, `TenantMembershipService::identityIsEstablished()`, and closes **all three** non-`Active` statuses together — `Invited`, `Declined` and `Removed` — rather than `Invited` alone: an admin who *removes* a member can otherwise re-adopt them at a role of their choosing by asserting the address, and a `Declined` invitation can otherwise be overridden. **(2)** Domain-ownership verification — the structural fix — is **rejected as this increment's shape** and filed as its own row instead: it is a new table, a DNS challenge, an admin surface and a grandfathering decision for every live connection, and the takeover would stay live for all of it. This narrows the hole; it does not replace that row. **(3)** Scope is the `major` **plus the two cheap siblings on the same door** — `decline()`'s missing identity question and `Show.vue`'s always-403 Accept button with its now-misleading `needsRegistration` prop — and **not** the `users.password_set_at` row, which edits `app/Actions/Fortify/`, needs a backfill and moves `E2eSeeder`'s invitation fixture.
     ➕ **CLAIM EXTENDED 2026-08-24, MID-BUILD, FOR ONE FILE M9 DID NOT SET OUT TO TOUCH: `tests/Feature/Audit/AuditLogPageTest.php`.** M9's first CI run went **5/6 with a failure that is not M9's** — `it offers every visible member as an actor filter` on run `32706386985`. ⚠️ **IT IS A LATENT FLAKE THAT HAS NOTHING TO DO WITH THIS DIFF, AND IT WAS DIAGNOSED RATHER THAN RE-RUN AWAY.** `AuditLogPresenter::actorOptions()` sorts `orderBy('name')`; the case pins `'Aaron Quiet'` as the first of two actors; and the other actor is `$this->owner = User::factory()->create()` — **a RANDOM faker name**. Faker's pool contains `Aaliyah`, `Aarav` and others that sort BEFORE `'Aaron Quiet'`, so the catalog's first entry becomes the owner and the property assertion fails with no bad code anywhere. **Reproduced deterministically** by seeding the owner as `'Aaliyah Faker'`, which produced CI's error message verbatim, and the file was restored byte-identical after. The fix pins the owner's name **at creation** rather than updating it afterwards — an UPDATE would run against `users`' own-row RLS policy and could match zero rows silently, which is this repository's most-repeated write trap. ⛔ **A GATE THAT IS RED ON A DICE ROLL IS WORSE THAN A MISSING ONE, because it teaches people to re-run until green** — which is exactly what would have buried this.

     ⚠️⚠️ **VERIFYING THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE FOUND THREE THINGS — TWENTY-EIGHT-FOR-TWENTY-EIGHT. (1) THE ROW NAMES ONE CALL SITE AND THERE ARE TWO, AND THE SECOND IS THE UI.** `InvitationController::prepareAcceptingUser()` forks the *authorisation* on `email_verified_at`, and **`show()` forks the *rendered page* on the identical predicate** (`'needsRegistration' => $user !== null && $user->email_verified_at === null`). So the page and the controller agree — consistently wrongly — and an enrolled member's invite page renders a *"Choose a password"* form. Fixing only `:99` would leave the page inviting a credential the server then refuses. **Grep the SHAPE, never the file the row names**, for the fifth increment running. **(2) THE SAME COLUMN MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS IN TWO PATHS, AND THAT IS THE ROOT CONFUSION RATHER THAN A SECOND BUG.** `GoogleSignInProvisioner:176` also reads `email_verified_at === null`, and there it is **correct** — ADR-0019 §D4's *"link only onto an already-verified account"*, i.e. *"do not trust this identity"*. The invitation path reads the same column as *"this identity does not exist yet"*. **Checked and deliberately not changed**; naming it is the point, because the fix must not be a tree-wide sweep of a column that legitimately means different things in different places. **(3) THE FIX HAS AN `tests/e2e/` CONSEQUENCE, FOUND ONLY BECAUSE THE E2E TREE WAS GREPPED FIRST.** `auth-axe.spec.ts:76` scans `/invitations/<token>` for the `E2eSeeder` invite, and that seeder's `$pending` is created **`verified: false` WITH A KNOWN PASSWORD** (`E2eSeeder.php:201`). Under any honest *"has this identity ever been used"* predicate that fixture **stops being a registration case**, and `Show.vue` — which has **no else branch** — would render the Accept button alone, changing the shape of the page the axe gate scans.
     **THE DESIGN DECISION, WITH THE REJECTED ALTERNATIVE RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE TEMPTING ONE.** **Taken:** fork on *"has this identity ever been used"* — a usable password, a confirmed second factor, or any prior membership — rather than on `email_verified_at`, at **both** `show()` and the accept handler, and route anyone who has to the sign-in-then-accept hand-off `prepareAcceptingUser()`'s own docblock already calls *"Increment C"*. **Rejected:** narrowing the predicate to *"a confirmed factor or an active membership elsewhere"* so the e2e fixture keeps its current meaning and the axe page never moves. It closes the reported hole and leaves the adjacent one open — an unverified account with a real password would still have that password overwritten by a token-holder — and **choosing a predicate to protect a fixture is choosing the fixture over the defect.** The fixture is what moves: `E2eSeeder`'s pending invitee becomes a genuine placeholder (no password), which is what the row it models actually is in production.
     ⚠️ **AND `Show.vue` IS NOT TOUCHED, WHICH IS WHAT KEEPS THIS OUT OF LANE A.** `resources/js/Pages/invitations/Show.vue` is Lane A's column; it needs no change because it already renders the correct page for `needsRegistration: false` — the same page an already-verified invitee sees today. **The server-side predicate is the whole fix.** If that turns out to be wrong mid-build, the Vue file is claimed here rather than edited on the strength of "it is only a few lines".

Tests follow their sources: `tests/Feature/Tenancy/MembershipRoutesTest.php` · `tests/Feature/Tenancy/MembershipLifecycleTest.php`. ⚠️ **`resources/js/Pages/invitations/Show.vue` IS CLAIMED BUT IS EXPECTED TO GO UNEDITED** — recorded so Lane A can see the reservation, and to be released explicitly if unused.
     ⛔ **NO ADR NUMBER — `0021` STAYS FREE**, `0010` stays reserved for H1d, and ADR-0016's next free sub-decision is `§D33`: this is a defect fix against `docs/multi-tenancy-rbac-design.md` §7's existing invitation design, not a new decision. ⛔ **MIGRATION PREFIX: none expected — SUPERSEDED, SEE THE SECOND AMENDMENT BELOW.** The fix is a predicate, not schema; if one proves necessary it is `2026_08_17_000107` and this line is amended before it is written. **It proved necessary: `2026_08_17_000107` IS SPENT.**
     ➕ **CLAIM AMENDED 2026-08-20, BEFORE ANY CODE, BECAUSE THE PREDICATE THIS CLAIM CHOSE IS PARTLY UNAVAILABLE — LESSON (c), *THE FIX A ROW NAMES MAY NOT EXIST*, ON THE CLAIM'S OWN FIRST HOUR.** *"Has a usable password"* cannot be asked: `resolveOrCreateUser()` gives a placeholder `Hash::make(Str::random(48))` and `users.password` is **NOT NULL**, so a placeholder's hash is indistinguishable from a real one — **the identical indistinguishability ADR-0016 §D22 already records** for its own fork (*"a hash of 64 random bytes is indistinguishable from a hash of a real password"*), which means this repository has now paid for that fact twice. ⚠️ **AND THE OBVIOUS SUBSTITUTE IS ALSO WRONG:** `tos_accepted_at` looks like a *"this account has been used"* stamp and is written by **exactly one** writer — `InvitationController` itself — so a self-registered member has it NULL and SSO JIT deliberately leaves it NULL (ADR-0016's provisioning note). Using it would refuse the very people it is meant to admit. **THE PREDICATE IS THEREFORE BUILT ONLY FROM OBSERVABLE FACTS**: `email_verified_at`, `two_factor_confirmed_at`, `google_id` (ADR-0019 §D1) or **any `tenant_users` row other than this invite** — i.e. *"this identity carries a credential or a history beyond the pending invite"*. Each is a positive record of something that happened, which is the property `password` lacks. **The rescope is recorded here, before the code, rather than discovered in review.**
     ⚠️ **REPRODUCE BEFORE FIXING, THEN PROVE THE FIX BY STASHING IT** — the defect is already reproduced (an unauthenticated `POST /invitations/{token}` for a seeded enrolled-and-unverified member answered a redirect to `/dashboard` with that member authenticated), so the owed artefact is the committed inverse: a case asserting the takeover is **refused**, which `git stash push -- app/` must turn red.
     ➕➕ **CLAIM AMENDED A SECOND TIME 2026-08-20, STILL BEFORE ANY CODE — AND THIS ONE SPENDS A MIGRATION PREFIX THE CLAIM SAID IT WOULD NOT NEED.** Verifying the rescoped predicate against the schema found that its FOURTH signal — *"any `tenant_users` row other than this invite"* — **cannot be asked today**. `tenant_users` carries the **strict** RLS shape plus `unique(tenant_id, user_id)` (`create_tenant_users_table.php`, *"at most one membership record per tenant, ever"*), so inside the invited tenant that query is **empty by construction: the invite row IS the only row**. And the cross-RLS hop this controller already uses cannot reach it either — `apply_users_rls.php` grants the pre-auth role exactly `GRANT SELECT, UPDATE ON users` and **nothing else**, with no grant and no policy on `tenant_users`. ⛔ **SO `2026_08_17_000107` IS CLAIMED AND SPENT HERE** (`apply_auth_role_read_to_tenant_users` — `GRANT SELECT ON tenant_users TO meridian_auth` plus a role-scoped permissive `tenant_users_auth_select ... TO meridian_auth USING (true)`, generated by `TenantIsolation` rather than hand-written per ADR-0002 §D2), **and Lane B's block resumes at `2026_08_17_000108`.** The `MIGRATION PREFIX: none expected` line above is amended by this paragraph exactly as it instructed, rather than rewritten out of the record. ⚠️ **AND THE CLAUSE IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN DECORATIVE, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH A MIGRATION: IT CARRIES THE LIKELIEST VICTIM.** `SsoUserProvisioner` stamps `email_verified_at => now()` and `GoogleSignInProvisioner` stamps **both** `google_id` and `email_verified_at`, so the three column signals already cover every SSO and every Google identity. What they do **not** cover is the ordinary case — **a verified member with no second factor who changes their email address**: `UpdateUserProfileInformation::updateVerifiedUser()` force-fills `email_verified_at => null` on any email change, and `two_factor_confirmed_at` and `google_id` are NULL for them. Dropping the clause would have shipped a fix that closes the row's TITLE and misses its largest sub-case. **The fork was put to the user with the two alternatives priced (a new `users.password_set_at` positive column, which is strictly more complete but edits `app/Actions/Fortify/` and moves the e2e invitation fixture; or the three columns alone, which is cheapest and incomplete) and they chose the membership carve-out on 2026-08-20.** The cost is stated rather than buried: the pre-auth role gains a deployment-wide **membership** read on top of the deployment-wide **identity** read `users_auth_select ... USING (true)` already gives it, so RBAC §9's *"no user-supplied predicate may ever run on `pgsql_auth`"* is the whole guard and this query's predicate is a server-derived uuid with exact equality. ⛔ **`0021` STILL STAYS FREE** — ADR-0002 §D3 owns database roles and RLS carve-outs and is **amended in place** on the P2c / ADR-0016 §D31 / P3a precedent; `0010` stays reserved for H1d; ADR-0016's next free sub-decision is still `§D33`. ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE PREDICATE'S MEMBERSHIP CLAUSE NEEDS THE CONJUNCT `joined_at IS NOT NULL`, WHICH IS A CORRECTNESS REQUIREMENT AND NOT A NARROWING.** `resolveOrCreateUser()` creates exactly ONE placeholder per email address, so a genuinely-new person invited to two workspaces holds **two `Invited` rows** — a bare *"another row exists"* test would mark that placeholder established and lock them out of **both** password-setting arms, a regression manufactured by the fix itself. `joined_at` is the positive record of *having actually been a member somewhere*, which is the property the predicate is asking about. ✅ **AND THIS CLAIM'S OWN FINDING (3) IS VOID — ITS FIRST AMENDMENT KILLED IT, AND SAYING SO IS WHY THE E2E TREE DOES NOT MOVE.** Finding (3) held that `E2eSeeder`'s `$pending` *"stops being a registration case"* and this claim decided *"the fixture is what moves"*. That was true only of the *"has a usable password"* predicate the FIRST amendment withdrew as unavailable. Under the predicate actually being built, `$pending` has no `email_verified_at`, no `two_factor_confirmed_at`, no `google_id` and no joined membership, so it stays `needsRegistration: true`, the axe page shape is unchanged, and `AuthScanFixturesTest`'s fixture pins stay green. **`database/seeders/E2eSeeder.php`, `tests/e2e/auth-axe.spec.ts` and `resources/js/Pages/invitations/Show.vue` are hereby RELEASED UNEDITED** — the reservations stay recorded so Lane A can see what was held and returned, and the option to re-take them stays open if the build proves otherwise. This matters because **the e2e suite cannot run on this host**, so an avoided `tests/e2e/` edit is a risk that is not taken rather than a risk that is measured. ⚠️ **TWO DEBTS THE EDIT CREATES, BOTH FILED HERE BEFORE THEY CAN BE FORGOTTEN.** (1) **`users.last_active_tenant_id` reads exactly like a fifth signal and is DEAD** — zero writers anywhere in `app/`, its only three references being description strings in `TenantExtractColumns`. Not used, not fixed, and filed as its own `minor` backlog row, because a deliberately-unfixed finding that lives only in a plan is invisible to every later search. (2) **THREE PROSE SITES BECOME FALSE THE MOMENT THE GRANT LANDS, AND NONE OF THEM WILL GO RED** — `tests/Feature/Auth/TwoFactorChallengeTest.php` (twice) and `tests/Feature/Auth/CentralHostLoginTest.php` each state *"`meridian_auth` holds grants on `users` alone"* in a comment, and `apply_users_rls.php`'s own docblock says *"on `users` only"*. They are comments rather than assertions, which is precisely why they must be found by grep and corrected — M7's lesson (b) applied to an edit that GROWS the privilege rather than the file.

(1) THE ROW'S OWN EVIDENCE HAD GONE STALE, IN EXACTLY THE WAY THE ROW IS ABOUT.** It cites `0016:168`; the sentence now sits at **`:238`**, because M2 inserted §D31 seventy lines above it (`git show 0028ea1~1:docs/adr/0016-saml-sso.md` puts it back at :168). A row about an unstable citation, carried by an unstable citation — which is the argument for the fix: **the sentence needs its own § heading so it can be cited stably**, 7(g)'s "cite the FILENAME, never the bare number" one level down. **(2) THE ROW NAMES FIVE MIS-CITING PASSAGES AND THERE ARE TWELVE, ACROSS SIX FILES — ONE THE SOURCE (§D11 ITSELF) AND ELEVEN THAT INHERITED IT** — lesson (c) for the fourth increment running; grep the SHAPE (`§D22`), never the file the row names. The six it does not name: `docs/adr/0019:37`, `docs/security-threat-model.md:163` and `:192`, `docs/ACCESS-MATRIX.md:397`, `app/Services/Auth/GoogleSessionStarter.php:23,:25`, `tests/Feature/Auth/GoogleSignInWebTest.php:839`. **(3) EIGHT *CORRECT* §D22 CITATIONS SIT IN THE SAME GREP AND A PATTERN SWEEP WOULD CORRUPT THEM** — `SsoStepUpController.php:19`, `SsoStepUpService.php:17`, `SsoAuthOutcome.php:13`, `2026_08_15_000001_…:11`, `routes/tenant.php:357`, `SsoStepUpWebTest.php:26`, `0016:240` and `0016:266` are all genuinely about step-up. **This is the ADR-0017 renumber shape from PR #153 in miniature: decided by reading each line, never by path.** `PROGRESS_ARCHIVE.md:2381,:2715` are archive and are not rewritten. **(4) `EnforceTenantTwoFactor`'s OPENING DOCBLOCK STATES A FALSE PREMISE THAT IS LOAD-BEARING FOR ITS OWN DESIGN ARGUMENT** — *"Fortify already challenges an enrolled user at login"* is untrue for the SSO door, which is the door this middleware most often guards. Its BEHAVIOUR is correct (it checks the enrolment flag, right regardless), so this is a comment fix. ⚠️⚠️ **(5) THE SHAPE-GREP FOR `Auth::login(` FOUND TWO MORE SESSION-MINTING DOORS, AND THIS INCREMENT'S FIRST READING OF ONE OF THEM WAS WRONG — OVERTURNED BY ITS OWN ADVERSARIAL PASS AND CORRECTED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DELETED.** `ImpersonationSessionController` is correctly exempt (the operator's own unconditional MFA plus step-up is the authority). `InvitationController` was cleared on the reasoning *"an already-verified invitee must already be authenticated; a placeholder has no confirmed factor"* — **both halves true, and neither is the branch**: `prepareAcceptingUser()` forks on **`email_verified_at`**, and **enrolled-but-unverified is an ordinary reachable state** (an email change nulls `email_verified_at`, and nothing in `app/` ever clears `two_factor_confirmed_at`). The unverified arm skips the file's only identity check, force-fills an attacker-chosen password and `Auth::login()`s — a live `major`, filed and reproduced. ⚠️ **A DOOR SURVEY IS ONLY AS GOOD AS THE PREDICATE IT CHECKS EACH DOOR AGAINST, AND WRITING "checked, not a defect" INTO THE PERMANENT RECORD IS WHAT MAKES A WRONG PREDICATE DURABLE.** The grep did its job; the reading of one result did not. **(6) THE SAML POLARITY IS ASSERTED BY NO TEST AT ALL** — `tests/Feature/Sso/` greps zero for `two_factor_confirmed_at`, `two-factor-challenge` and `twoFactor`, while `GoogleSignInWebTest:839` pins the Google side. A future "consistency fix" flipping SAML to challenge would pass the whole suite green, which is what makes a test the substance of this increment rather than decoration. ⚠️⚠️ **(7) THE ONE THING THIS INCREMENT COULD HAVE BROKEN, CHECKED — AND THEN BROKEN ANYWAY, IN THE EXACT CLASS IT EXISTS TO FIX.** The pre-existing by-line citations into ADR-0016 were checked and all sat above the insertion point. What that check did not cover is the citations **this diff itself wrote**: a +59-line §D32 and a +10-line middleware docblock invalidated M7's own `0016:238`, its `0019:37` and `ACCESS-MATRIX.md:397` evidence list, **and two LIVE rows in `docs/feature-backlog.md` that M7 never read** — including `EnforceTenantTwoFactor.php:33-52`, the sole evidence for the *enrolment-nudge* argument §D32 leans on twice. Caught by the adversarial pass and re-anchored on **quoted text and symbol names instead of line numbers**. ⚠️ **AN INCREMENT ABOUT UNSTABLE CITATIONS PRODUCED FOUR OF THEM. "Which citations does my edit invalidate" MUST INCLUDE THE ONES THE EDIT IS ADDING, and the durable answer is to stop citing by line where an anchor will do.**
     **THE DECISION, AND IT IS THE USER'S RATHER THAN THIS INCREMENT'S (2026-08-20).** The fork — ratify the as-built behaviour, or make SAML challenge a personal TOTP the way Google does — was brought and answered **ratify as-built**. Three things make that the right reading rather than a convenience: **§D22 already decides the same polarity for step-up** (an SSO session re-proves at the IdP with `ForceAuthn="true"`, never against a local credential), so login is the same principle at the same door; `docs/security-threat-model.md:192` already records the SAML side as *"a deliberate divergence for the person's [control]"*; and the **2026-08-14 Google decision was itself framed as a divergence FROM this**, so the polarity was in front of the product owner then. **Rejected:** making `SsoLoginCompletionController` fork like `GoogleSessionStarter` — it overrides an enterprise trust anchor a workspace administrator chose with a factor they did not configure, and contradicts §D22's mechanism; and building the per-workspace *"our IdP already performs MFA"* setting now — a new `SettingKey`, a migration and a settings control, materially larger than a row filed as a documentation defect. It stays what both ADRs already call it: the **revisit trigger**.
     **SCOPE, DECIDED RATHER THAN DRIFTED INTO.** This increment takes the row **plus one `minor` sibling** — `docs/feature-backlog.md:1102`, *ADR-0019 is the sole `Proposed` ADR in the directory for a decision that is ratified and fully built* — because it is one word in a file this diff already rewrites at `:23` and `:28`, on the same subject: **ADR-0019 does not accurately record what was decided.** ⛔ **NOT TAKEN, AND FILED RATHER THAN SILENTLY DROPPED**: `feature-backlog.md:1106`'s stale-by-line-citation cluster (a dozen files, its own row, unrelated mechanism); and **a lint gate that would catch a `§D<n>` citation naming a section whose text does not support it** — real, and out of scope because it touches `scripts/` and moves a lint-gate baseline. **It gets its own backlog row in this diff**, per the standing rule that a deliberately-unfixed finding is filed the moment the decision is made.

     ⚠️⚠️ **VERIFYING THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE FOUND THREE THINGS — TWENTY-SIX-FOR-TWENTY-SIX. (1) THE ROW NAMES ONE PATH AND THERE ARE TWO, WHICH IS M3's SHAPE EXACTLY.** It blames `sweep()`, but `ensureFresh()` is called from **`DeliverConnectorMessageJob:124`**, and that job is *also* a `TenantAwareJob` — so H16a's pre-flight refresh rotates the token inside the DELIVERY job's transaction too. It is the **worse** of the two: the sweep does nothing after the refresh but refresh the next connection, whereas the delivery job goes on to make up to three more outbound calls, each of which can throw or eat the 60s `$timeout`. Fixing only the named path would leave the row's own sentence true and the defect live. **(2) THE TRANSACTION IS LOAD-BEARING AND CANNOT SIMPLY BE REMOVED**: `TenantContext::applyLocal()` issues `SET LOCAL`, so RLS scoping dies with the transaction — which is why the fix is a per-connection commit SEAM (M3's own `TenantContext::runFor()` is the shape) rather than "move the write out". **(3) THE BATCHING MULTIPLIES IT**: `sweep()` (`:53-72`) walks every due connection for a tenant inside the one transaction, so a 60s timeout does not lose one rotated token, it loses **every** token rotated in that run.
     **SCOPE, DECIDED RATHER THAN DRIFTED INTO.** This increment takes the row **and its `major` sibling directly below it** — *`ensureFresh()` takes no lock, so two concurrent deliveries destroy a healthy Airtable grant* — because they are **one mechanism, not two**: both are answered by making "refresh one connection" a single locked, immediately-committed critical section, and shipping them separately means the second increment rewrites the first's code. ⛔ **The third sibling stays filed**: *the setup-time directory has no pre-flight refresh* (`TabularDestinationDirectory.php:46,68`) is a different call site with a different fix, and folding it in would be scope drift.

     ⚠️⚠️ **VERIFYING THE ROW AGAINST THE CODE MOVED IT TWICE — TWENTY-FIVE-FOR-TWENTY-FIVE, AND THE FIRST MOVE IS THAT THE ROW'S OWN FIX DOES NOT EXIST.** **(1) "Send an idempotency token" presumes a provider that accepts one, and NEITHER DOES.** Google Sheets `values.append` has no request-id or idempotency header; Airtable's create-record endpoint has none either. A header both providers ignore would *look* like a fix and change nothing — the same shape as the e2e overflow assertion three files describe as working. The only thing that can settle *did the first attempt land?* is **the destination itself**, so the fix is a **read-back reconciliation on the retry**, not a token on the write. **(2) THE TRIGGER IS NARROWER THAN THE ROW SAYS, AND THAT NARROWNESS IS WHAT MAKES A FIX SAFE.** A duplicate needs an *indeterminate* outcome — the request left, the provider may have committed, no answer came back — which is only the `ConnectionException` path. Every arm that produced an HTTP **status** is determinate: a 429, 403, 422 or 5xx is a *response*. Today all of them collapse into one `failed()` and the ladder re-drives them alike. Ten duplicates would need ten consecutive committed-but-unanswered writes; the realistic count is **two**. **(3) THE REACH OF ANY RECONCILIATION IS BOUNDED BY THE TENANT'S OWN MAPPING, AND THE ROW DOES NOT SAY SO.** `SubmissionRowProjector::META_SUBMISSION_ID` is an **optional** column offered by `MappableColumnCatalog`; a rule that does not bind it writes nothing identifying the submission, so no probe can find it — and **both test fixtures bind it by default** (`airtableMapping()` / `sheetsMapping()`), which is M4's trap exactly: every new case would otherwise pass on a property of the fixture. The unbound case gets its own test and its own filed row.
     **THE DESIGN DECISION, WITH THE REJECTED HALVES RECORDED BECAUSE THE NEXT READER WILL THINK OF THEM.** **Taken:** an unknown-outcome write is remembered on the delivery row, and the next attempt **reconciles before it writes** — it asks the destination whether this submission is already there, keyed on `__submission_id`. Present ⇒ delivered, no write. Absent ⇒ write. Cannot ask ⇒ exactly today's behaviour. **Rejected — dead-letter the indeterminate delivery instead:** it never duplicates, and it trades a rare duplicate for a rare **silent loss**, because there is no manual redeliver affordance (`DeliverConnectorMessageJob::$skipMeter` is explicitly *"reserved for a future manual redeliver"*). A duplicate is visible in the tenant's own sheet and deletable; a row never written is invisible and unrecoverable through the product. **Rejected — a fuzzy exact-row match so the unbound case is covered too:** its false positive IS that silent loss, and two respondents giving identical answers is ordinary on a short form. **Identity-only, or nothing.** **Rejected — treating 5xx as indeterminate:** it would route the far more common arm through an extra read to guard a case both APIs' contracts say does not happen. Filed as a residual instead.

The defect is precisely *no context → zero rows, silently*, so the fix is aimed at that state and the reproduction pins it rather than a mismatch that would have failed anyway. **(4) THE FIX FALSIFIES A CLAIM REPEATED IN NINE DOCBLOCKS.** `grep "null tenant GUC"` finds nine sites, **seven** of them dispatch listeners giving *"a queued listener would run under a null tenant GUC"* as the reason they are not `ShouldQueue`. A guard whose correctness depends on a non-local invariant in another class is the shape being removed, not one to add. ⛔ **AND ONE THING DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: THE SIX LISTENERS ARE NOT FLIPPED TO `ShouldQueue`.** That is a behaviour change owed its own increment; it is filed in `docs/feature-backlog.md` in this same increment rather than left invisible, which is the J4b1 lesson.
