     **THE DESIGN DECISION, TAKEN RATHER THAN ASKED, AND THE REJECTED HALF IS RECORDED BECAUSE THE NEXT READER WILL THINK OF IT.** **Taken:** refuse when the roll-up says no stored certificate is usable — one check ahead of the sequence in `consumeAssertion()`, which is §D11's rule verbatim (*“any currently-valid certificate means the connection is fine”*). **Rejected:** narrowing `x509certMulti.signing` to the currently-valid subset, which would also stop an expired *sibling* signing while a valid one is present. Two grounds. **(a) It makes clock skew into an availability control** — a not-yet-valid successor is minutes away by design during a rollover, and §D11 exists precisely to stop a healthy rollover pair being read as a fault. **(b) The residual it would close is already closed by §D10** — metadata is imported as a whole half, atomically, so a re-import *removes* the retired certificate from the set; the dangerous state is “nobody re-imported”, which is expired-only, which the taken design refuses. The narrowing that survives is written into the threat model as the reduced residual rather than left implied. ⛔ **TWO THINGS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED, SO THE NEXT READER DOES NOT GO LOOKING:** the mint side (`SsoLoginController`) still mints — refusing there reaches the same 404 by a second route and puts a second copy of the rule in a second file; and `SsoGate::activeConnection()` still returns the connection — making a dead anchor look like “no SSO” would hand `RequireRecentPassword` a graceful password fallback **and 404 the ACS with no recorded reason**, destroying the one diagnostic this row exists to produce.

(2) `accept()` writes neither an audit row nor an event, so the `('tenant_users', created)` row covers only the three self-serve doors and misses the commonest one. (3) `('submission', updated)` is written by **two** services — `SubmissionReviewService::apply()` and `SubmissionAnswerEditService::edit()` — so review and edit are indistinguishable by `(auditable_type, event)` and need a structural discriminator on the payload. **So the backfill's second source is `tenant_users`** (`invited_by` / `invited_at` / `joined_at` — the authority on membership), and that is §D10's first half. ⚠️ Note also that `member.joined`'s `subject_id` is the **USER** id while the audit row's `auditable_id` is the **MEMBERSHIP** uuid: keying on the wrong one produces a duplicate award the unique index cannot catch, because the subject differs.

It is fixed here rather than filed, because the alternative is a scoreboard that permanently disagrees with itself: the backfill would grant every historical invited member their join points and the very next acceptance would grant none. ⚠️ **The side effect is stated rather than discovered** — `accept()` raising `MemberJoined` also sends the existing Owner/Admin `member_joined` notification, which is correct product behaviour (today the Owner is told when a stranger self-registers but *not* when their own invitee accepts) and whose copy already reads correctly for that case. **Seeded fixture movement is MEASURED with a real `migrate:fresh --seed`, never forecast** — that was K1b's biggest miss and this increment moves the fixtures deliberately.

8. **RUN THE PREFLIGHT — the traps in these rules are now executable (added 2026-08-28, M36).**
   `php scripts/preflight.php --lane=a|b` at session open, and again with `--with-gates --with-pint`
   before you push. It exits non-zero on anything blocking.

   **WHY IT EXISTS, AND WHY IT IS NOT A NEW RULE SO MUCH AS AN OLD ONE MADE ENFORCEABLE.** Everything
   it checks was already written down here — Rule 7(a)'s branch base, 7(g)'s *a claim is a pushed
   commit*, 7(c)'s concurrent-suite hazard, the `PROGRESS.md` splice warnings — and every one of them
   was hit again anyway, because prose has to be re-read, re-interpreted, and remembered at the right
   moment by whoever holds the tree. **A check does not have to be remembered.** These rules are now
   roughly four thousand words per lane, which is the argument rather than an aside.

   ⛔ **IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT A `ci.yml` STEP AND NOT IN `composer run quality`.** Most of what it
   asserts is meaningless in CI — no worktrees, no local Pest, the claim already merged. The header of
   `scripts/preflight.php` says so, so that a later reader does not "fix" it by registering it.

   **TWO COMPANIONS SHIP WITH IT.** `scripts/mutate.php` is the positive-control harness — use it for
   every mutation rather than hand-rolling one, because it takes its tokens from FILES (no shell can
   eat a dollar sign), aborts unless the sha256 MOVES, prints the mutated line, refuses to run beside a
   live Pest, requires a green baseline first, and restores by byte comparison. `scripts/gate-baselines.php`
   generates `docs/gate-baselines.md` from a named CI run.

   ⛔ **AND THAT LAST ONE CHANGES WHAT A HAND-OFF MAY CONTAIN: GATE NUMBERS LIVE IN
   `docs/gate-baselines.md` AND A HAND-OFF THAT RESTATES THEM IS THE DEFECT.** Same reasoning as
   7(b)'s lane boundary — one authority, referenced rather than copied — and the same evidence: M30
   found two stale figures in one hand-off, and M36 found the Lane A line quoting Pest at 4595/19,433
   when the run reported **4611/19465**, one increment later.

Full record — the sixth reachable kind the row never named, the fixture the fix silently invalidated, the wrong base whose failure looked exactly like a broken test, and the shared-worktree finding: Standing Rule 7(g)'s released-claim entry.
- **🅱️ LANE B — `M29` IS MERGED (PR #219, `7892f7f`, CI 6/6 GREEN WITH REAL STEP COUNTS): THE SCREENSHOT'S GATE HAD NO TEST, AND THE CENSUS FOUND A SIBLING ROUTE WALKING AROUND IT.**
  ⛔⛔ **IT NEARLY DID NOT MERGE, AND THE REASON IS WORTH MORE THAN THE MERGE.** GitHub Actions ran nothing for this account for the better part of an hour: the first run **failed** after 55s with all six jobs at `status: queued` and **`steps: []`** — the no-runner-ran signature — and the re-run, plus both of Lane A's M30 runs, then sat queued **30–57 minutes** with an empty job list.   ⛔⛔ **AND THE CI NUMBER SETTLED A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN THE TWO LANES' QUOTED BASELINES.** CI Pest is **4564 / 19,345 (2 pre-existing warnings)**. M29 adds exactly **seven** tests, and **4557 + 7 = 4564** — the post-M26 figure lands on the nose, so Lane A's quoted post-M28 baseline of **4544 was 13 low** and M25/M27/M28 added no PHP tests that could explain a fall. **Anyone measuring a delta against 4544 would have read a phantom `+20`.** ⚠️ **The local full-suite number is NOT the CI number**: local was `4229 / 17,970`, a 335-test gap on the same tree. The row asked for a DENY test on `GET /feedback/{report}/screenshot`, which had none anywhere in `tests/`. It was true, and it was the smaller half.
  ⛔⛔ **A GATE IS ONLY AS NARROW AS THE WIDEST ROUTE THAT REACHES THE SAME BYTES, AND THIS ONE WAS LIVE RATHER THAN LATENT.** `GET /attachments/{attachment}` is authorized by `AttachmentPolicy::view()`, whose entire body was `$user->can('submissions.view')` — **it never touched its `$attachment` argument**. ADR-0015 §D6 filed the screenshot into that same shared table; `RolePermissionSeeder` grants `submissions.view` to `viewer`, `reviewer` and `form_editor` and grants `feedback.view` to **none** of them. So the id-addressed sibling served the PII image to exactly the three roles the dedicated route refuses — while `FeedbackController.php:59-65` said in its own words that it declined to route through `AttachmentController` *precisely to avoid that coupling*. The coupling it refused was open in the other direction the whole time.
  ⛔ **THE CENSUS UNIT HAS TO BE THE RESOURCE, NOT THE FEATURE — THIS IS M26'S LESSON AT ONE MORE REMOVE.** M26 learned to enumerate *consumers of a field* from the code rather than the report. Not enough here: "who serves this screenshot" finds two routes and stops. What found the defect was enumerating **every endpoint in the repository that serves stored bytes — ten** — and asking of each *which test asserts a refusal*. **Four of ten had one.** Two routes reaching the same bytes under two different permissions is invisible to any sweep organised by feature.
  ⚠️ **`AttachmentPolicy` HAD NO TEST OF ANY KIND, AND NO HTTP TEST ANYWHERE DROVE THAT ROUTE.** `AttachmentRlsTest` is four DB-level cases; every other `attachments` hit under `tests/` is `TenantUrl` string-building. The only role gate on the whole authenticated media read path was unasserted. Fixed with a `match` on the kind — `feedback.view` predates Phase 0, so **no key was minted and no paired file moved**.
  ⚠️ **THE ROW'S OWN PROPOSED FIX WAS HALF-INERT, MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** It asks for a Viewer `assertForbidden` **plus** a cross-tenant `assertNotFound`. `bootstrap/app.php:217-218` runs `SubstituteBindings` **before** `Authorize`, so the cross-tenant case 404s at binding and **passes unchanged with the middleware deleted** — mutation 3 removed it and reddened the same-tenant Viewer case **alone**. Both ship; the cross-tenant one now says in a comment that it is not a substitute.
  ⛔⛔ **THREE MUTATIONS, PAIRWISE DISJOINT RED SETS — AND THE HARNESS FAILED FIRST IN THE WAY HARDEST TO NOTICE.** Baseline 19/84 green; mutation 1 (policy stops reading the kind) **1 red**; mutation 2 (default arm narrows) **2 red**, one of them the named positive control; mutation 3 (middleware off the route) **1 red**. ⚠️ **v1 OF THE HARNESS SPLICED BY INDEX ARITHMETIC AND PRODUCED SYNTACTICALLY INVALID PHP** — every test would have errored, **which reads as "the mutation was detected" if you only count red**. v2 replaces one literal token and adds two checks the standing rule does not yet name: **`php -l` the mutant, and assert its sha256 actually moved.**
  ⚠️ **`TaskStop` ON A BACKGROUND SHELL IS NOT `kill` ON WHAT THAT SHELL SPAWNED.** Stopping the harness killed the tool's wrapper and **not** the `bash` script, which kept running and mutating; its `docker exec` host side died while the container's Pest kept going, and a **new PID appeared after the first kill**. Three kills — wrapper, host script, container process — before the tree could be restored.
  ⚠️ **A NAMESPACE HANDED OVER IN A NEXT-PROMPT IS A RESERVATION, NOT A DESTINATION.** The claim reserved **ADR-0016 §D35** because the hand-off named it. ADR-0016 is **SAML SSO**. **ADR-0015 §D6 created this coupling, §D7 already says "both screenshot routes", §D8 marks the row PII** — so the decision is **ADR-0015 §D9** and the reserved slot was never spent. **Seventh consecutive Lane B increment amending rather than minting; `0022` and `§D35` both stay free.**
  ➕ **SIX FINDINGS FILED, NOT FIXED, EACH THE MOMENT IT WAS DECIDED.** Largest: `AttachmentPolicy::view()` is **still flat where `SubmissionPolicy::view()` is scoped**, affecting exactly `form_editor` and `reviewer` (`hasOrgWideVisibility()` is `dashboard.org.view`, which owner, admin *and* viewer hold). ⛔ **REVOCATION DOES NOT REVOKE**: remove a `form_editor` from a form and `SubmissionPolicy` refuses them on the next request while every attachment id they ever saw keeps working. Deliberately not folded in — that is how §D6 produced this defect in the first place — and `AttachmentPolicyTest` asserts the current behaviour so the row cannot be closed by accident.

They passed locally only because `public/build` happened to exist. **These were I5 defects that CI had never once been allowed to run.**

   **THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS ENTRY SURVIVES ITS OWN CLOSURE: an unverified gate is not a green gate.** Local runs said 2757/2757 and clean audits on both trees; CI still found two genuine problems, one of them a live high-severity advisory that could not have existed at local-run time. **Do not merge to `main` on the strength of local gates alone**, however complete they look. **How to tell an outage from a real failure** — an outage-killed job reports `conclusion: cancelled` with **`steps: []`**; a real failure has steps with a failed one among them. Both at once: `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/runs/<id>/jobs --jq '.jobs[]|"\(.name): \(.conclusion) steps=\(.steps|length)"'`.

4. **⚠️ H16b MUST FIX THE REDIRECT-URI PORT, and the obvious fix is a trap.** The app emits `http://localhost/oauth/google_sheets/callback`; the user registered `http://localhost:8080/oauth/google_sheets/callback` (correct — Google accepts `http://localhost`, and `.env.example` has always documented the port-carrying local form). `ConnectorRedirector::centralHost()` returns `tenancy.central_domain` with NO port, deliberately — its own docblock argues it, because the redirect URI must match the provider console byte-for-byte and production carries no port. **DO NOT "fix" this by putting a port in `CENTRAL_DOMAIN`:** that env var feeds BOTH `tenancy.central_domain` and the `central_domains` array (`config/tenancy.php:30` and `:38`), and `Route::domain()` matches `Request::getHost()`, which STRIPS the port — so `localhost:8080` there silently deletes the super-admin console route, the connector callback route ITSELF, the platform-host guard and subdomain tenant identification. The fix is in `ConnectorRedirector` (derive the port from `app.url`) plus pinning `APP_URL` in `phpunit.xml`, which currently forces `CENTRAL_DOMAIN=meridian.test` while `APP_URL` inherits `localhost:8080` — the test env contradicts itself. Attempted during the #99 merge and REVERTED: it breaks ~10 asserted redirects in `ConnectorOAuthFlowTest` and does not belong in a merge commit (the I2 → I8 precedent).

Publishing later should be uneventful: `drive.file` is Google's *Non-sensitive* tier and needs no verification review — which is exactly why H16a chose it over `spreadsheets`.

**NEW LOCAL-GATE LESSON (2026-08-06, re-confirmed 2026-08-11):** **a green local Vitest is NOT proof that every test ran.** A worker occasionally fails to START in this Docker setup; when it does, that file's tests never run and never appear in the total, while the run still reports the remaining files as passed. Observed as 1284 → 1270 → 1292 across three runs of the same tree, and again in J1e as **79 then 83 against 90 files actually on disk**. **Check the FILE COUNT — it is 90 as of J1e** (`find resources packages -name '*.test.ts' -not -path '*/node_modules/*' | wc -l`) — not just pass/fail, use `--no-file-parallelism`, and treat CI as the authority.

**NEW PEST LESSON (2026-08-11):** **the full suite now exhausts the app container's 128M PHP limit** and dies with a fatal in `routes/tenant.php` partway through — which reads like a route bug and is not. Run it as `php -d memory_limit=2G vendor/bin/pest`, not `php artisan test`.

**⛔ PEST LESSON SUPERSEDED AND SHARPENED (J2c, 2026-08-11): THE LOCAL FULL-SUITE PEST RUN SILENTLY DROPS WHOLE TEST FILES, AND THAT — NOT `artisan test` vs `vendor/bin/pest` — IS THE ~321-TEST GAP J2b COULD NOT EXPLAIN.** Measured, not inferred: **330 `*Test.php` files exist on disk; a full `vendor/bin/pest --list-tests` collects only 285.** The 45 missing are overwhelmingly `tests/Feature/Forms/` — **40 of its 44 classes**, including `FormHubGateTest`, `FormHubPageTest`, `FormTabSetReachabilityTest` and both of J2c's new files. Run **that directory alone** and all 44 classes / **365 tests** collect; run it as part of `tests/Feature` and only 4 classes survive. It is **not** memory (identical at 2G and 6G), **not** a stale cache (none exists), and **not** deterministic across trees — `StructuralValidationGateTest` was uncollected on `ace9505` and collected one commit later, which is how the discrepancy surfaced at all.

**⚠️ THE J2b PROOF CHECKED THE WRONG INVARIANT, and it is worth saying plainly because it read as airtight.** "Collected 3051, executed 3051, so nothing was silently skipped" proves only that the runner *ran everything it collected*; it says nothing about whether collection *saw every file*. The companion check — `tests/Unit` + `tests/Feature` summing exactly to the collected total — was **self-referential**, since both halves come from the same incomplete collection. The static `it(`-declaration counts were sound and stay sound; they were simply measuring a different thing from the runner. **The correct local check is the FILE COUNT, exactly as for Vitest:** `find tests -name '*Test.php' | wc -l` (330) against the distinct classes in `--list-tests` (285).

**CONSEQUENCES, which change how a local Pest number may be reported.** (1) **A local full-suite absolute is meaningless and so is a local full-suite DELTA** — J2c's own run moved 3051→3067 (+16) while the increment added **23** cases, the 7-test difference being purely which files collection happened to include that run. (2) **CI is the sole authority** — it runs `php artisan test` (`ci.yml:189`) and reported **3370** on J2b, ~321 above the local figure, which is approximately the 336 tests `tests/Feature/Forms` alone loses locally. (3) **Verify an increment's own tests by running their files or directories DIRECTLY**, and report that; J2c's are green that way (Forms 10 + 8 + 8, Submissions inbox 16). (4) Root cause is **still open** — the missing block is alphabetically contiguous within the directory, which fits neither "stops at the first bad file" nor a suffix/exclude rule. **Do not report a local full-suite Pest total again until it is closed.**

**I7b (do not re-open):** **THE GENERIC SUPER-ADMIN BYPASS IS WRONG ON `audits`, AND IT IS ALWAYS ONE LINE AWAY.** `TenantIsolation::applySuperAdminBypass('audits', ['SELECT'])` would have shipped a working platform audit viewer immediately — and handed the operator **every tenant's complete history** (every form title, every reviewer's `returned_reason`, every membership change in the deployment) in order to display a handful of platform-settings rows. **The helper's gate is a DEFAULT, NOT A CONTRACT**: it is correct only where the base shape is `nullable_global` (the tenant slice is already public there) and wrong on anything strict or append-only. `audits` therefore took a new NAMED generator, **`TenantIsolation::platformRowsBypass()`**, emitting `audits_platform_select` with `… AND tenant_id IS NULL`. **The plan said HAND-WRITE that policy; ADR-0002 §D2 says policies are generated by a reusable helper "not hand-written per table", and the user chose the generator** — the narrowing was the real requirement, hand-writing was only the means. ADR-0002 §D3 now carries the amendment. **THE POLICY NAME IS `audits_platform_select`, DELIBERATELY OUTSIDE THE `audits_superadmin_%` PREFIX** the older bypass migration sweeps in its `down()`: a shared prefix lets an unrelated rollback drop this policy while its `GRANT SELECT` survives, and the page then renders **zero rows with no error** under copy saying nobody has ever performed a platform action — a plausible lie on a compliance surface. It also keeps a `pg_policies` grep for `%_superadmin_%` an honest answer to "where does the operator hold an unrestricted read". **MUTATION-VERIFIED, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT PROVED NOTHING WHILE LOOKING LIKE IT DID.** Mutating the LIVE database is useless — `RefreshDatabase` runs `migrate:fresh` and rebuilds the correct policy underneath you, so the suite stayed green on a deliberately broken database. **The migration FILE is what must be mutated.** Done properly: swapping the generator for `applySuperAdminBypass()` reddens three `PlatformAuditRlsTest` cases including "never lets the elevated role read a TENANT-scoped audit row"; removing `withoutGlobalScope(Audit::$tenantScope)` reddens 9 of 11 `PlatformAuditReadTest` cases. **`whereNull('tenant_id')` IS NOT REDUNDANT WITH THE POLICY** and is three things at once: the ORM half of the isolation, defence in depth, and the query plan — `audits_tenant_select` has no `TO` clause so it applies to the elevated role too, Postgres OR-composes same-command permissive policies, and only a user-level leakproof `IS NULL` lets the planner keep the ordered scan on `audits_tenant_recent_idx`. "The policy already does that" is exactly how it gets deleted. **TWO LIVE 500s WERE FOUND AND FIXED, both latent since H5a purely because no UI ever called those routes:** `GET /admin/tenants/not-a-uuid` reached Postgres as `where id = 'not-a-uuid'` on a uuid column → **SQLSTATE 22P02, a 500 not a 404** (fixed with `->whereUuid('tenant')`), and `plan_id=garbage` did the same through `exists:plans,id`. **`'uuid'` ALONE DOES NOT FIX THE SECOND ONE** — Laravel short-circuits an attribute only after an *implicit* rule fails, so the rule set must be `['required','bail','uuid','exists:plans,id']`. Both mutation-verified against the real 22P02. **`EntitlementService::forget()` IS CALLED ON BOTH SIDES OF `tenantSnapshot()`, AND THIS TRACKER'S OWN STATED REASON FOR IT WAS WRONG.** It said the memo would carry one tenant's plan "into the rest of the render"; that cannot happen — inertia-laravel calls `Inertia::share()` BEFORE the controller, and `currentPlan()` early-returns on a null tenant id before it ever consults the memo. The real reason is the TEST process: the service is bound `scoped()` and **only the queue worker calls `forgetScopedInstances()`**, so one instance survives every `$this->get()`/`$this->post()` in a Pest run, and a render→mutate→render test would otherwise assert a stale plan **in the passing direction**. **EVERY FAILURE MODE ON THE DETAIL PAGE IS SILENT.** Move any read out of the adopt-context transaction and `storage_bytes` reads 0, `active_seats` reads 0, the owner reads null, the plan reads null — nothing throws. The owner assertion and the "usage reads the right tenant" assertion exist for exactly that. **`MdsModal`'s slot is `actions`; `MdsCard`'s is `footer`** — using `#footer` on a modal renders the confirm buttons NOWHERE, silently, and a Vitest case is what caught it. **THE ADMIN NAV OVERFLOWS BELOW ~900px AND ALWAYS DID.** `.admin__bar` is a fixed-height 56px flex row and the breakpoint that reflows it was 480px; five nav links measure ~860px against a ~720px content box at 768px, and the four pre-I7b links were already ~767px. Nothing in CI can see it — `/admin/*` is excluded from the Playwright sweep because `superadmin.mfa` needs a TOTP. Breakpoint raised to 900px, and the coverage gap is now a named residual in the threat model. **`clearPlatformRows()` / `committedSuperAdmin()` MOVED FROM `PlatformSettingsWriteTest` INTO `tests/Pest.php`** — Pest loads every file into one process, so a second declaration is a fatal redeclare. **THE FIXTURE PURGE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING: audits → tenants → users.** `audits.tenant_id` is `nullOnDelete`, so deleting a fixture tenant first does not delete its audit rows — it PROMOTES them into permanent platform rows the viewer under test then displays, this run and every run after. The same mechanism is a real product consequence: hard-deleting a tenant moves its whole history onto `/admin/audit-log` (now recorded in data-dictionary §13 and the threat model). **TENANT-DETAIL TESTS USE ORDINARY FACTORIES, NOT I7a's COMMITTED FIXTURES** — nothing on that page elevates, so the committed apparatus would be pure cost, and registering `purgeCommittedFeedbackFixtures()` there would sweep `slug LIKE 'console-%'` across unrelated suites. **⚠️ LOCAL PHPSTAN ON THIS MACHINE IS BLIND TO ELOQUENT MODEL PROPERTIES AND REPORTS ~64 PHANTOM ERRORS; CI REPORTS `[OK] No errors` ON THE SAME TREE** — same larastan v3.10.0, same PHP 8.4, vendor synced to `composer.lock`. Every one is a `property.notFound` on a model or an `unresolvableType` downstream of one; confirmed with a two-method probe where the method touching a model property errored and the identical method without one did not. **Do not chase them and do not "fix" code to satisfy them — but do NOT trust a green local run either, and this is the half that cost real time.** The blindness cuts BOTH ways: an unresolvable model type degrades to `mixed`, and `mixed` SILENCES downstream errors. On the first push CI rejected three genuine errors this container had reported as clean — a nullsafe call on a non-nullable `Carbon`, a redundant `is_string()`, and an `array` passed where `list<string>|null` was required. **Local PHPStan on this machine is not a weak signal, it is not a signal at all.** Budget one CI round-trip for static analysis on any increment that touches model properties or array shapes derived from them. Measure the DELTA against the parent branch instead — `phpstan analyse --error-format=json` on each branch, diff by `basename|message`. I7b's measured delta was 0 — and CI still found 3, because the errors that mattered were the ones local could not compute at all. The tell is that the phantom errors concentrate on model property access; the hidden ones do too.

**I7a (do not re-open):** **THE SUPER-ADMIN RLS CARVE-OUT IS ONLY THE DATABASE HALF, AND THE OTHER HALF FAILS SILENTLY.** `BelongsToTenant` adds its OWN global scope — `where tenant_id = <context ?? NO_TENANT_SENTINEL>` — to every Eloquent query, independently of RLS. The console runs on the central host with **no** tenant context, so `listFeedback()`/`findFeedback()` asked the database for rows belonging to a tenant that cannot exist: an empty list, no error, under an empty state reading "No feedback yet". Both now call `withoutGlobalScope(FeedbackReport::$tenantScope)`. **`listAllUsers()` escaped this only because `users` has no tenant column**, so every FUTURE console surface over a tenant-scoped table inherits the requirement. The test half matters just as much: `FeedbackRlsTest` drops the ORM scope too, because leaving it on would make those assertions pass whether the Postgres policies existed or not — a bypass written without its `TO meridian_superadmin` clause would have sailed straight through a green suite. **ROUTE-MODEL BINDING CANNOT BE USED ON A CONSOLE ROUTE OVER AN RLS TABLE** for the same root cause: binding resolves on the app connection, so every valid id 404s. `/admin/feedback/{feedback}` takes a raw uuid and resolves through the service; `/admin/tenants/{tenant}` gets away with binding only because `tenants` is RLS-exempt. **THE BYPASS IS `['SELECT']` AND MUST STAY THAT WAY.** Transitions deliberately do NOT elevate — the console already knows the report's tenant, so `transitionFeedback()` uses the H4 adopt-context pattern, which is the ONLY reason the audit row lands in the **reporting workspace's own** ledger where RBAC §9 says it must be visible. An UPDATE bypass would write `tenant_id = NULL` and hide the operator's handling from exactly the people entitled to see it. **`attachments` HAS NO BYPASS AT ALL, DELIBERATELY** — the console reads a screenshot by adopting the report's tenant context; a bypass there would hand the operator every respondent file and every OCR scan in the deployment in order to display one image. `FeedbackRlsTest` asserts both absences by querying `pg_policies`, so a later "for symmetry" addition fails there first. **THE AUDIT ALIAS IS THE PLURAL `feedback_reports`** — matching `AuditRedactor::PII` and `AuditableTypes::LABELS`, unlike the singular `submission`/`attachment`. The singular writes a perfectly valid-looking row while silently switching redaction OFF for `remarks`/`browser_info`. **CREATION IS NOT AUDITED AND THAT IS THE SPEC'S ANSWER**, not a judgement call: audit spec §1 registers exactly one event for this table, `updated`. **`Tenant::on('pgsql_privileged')` DOES NOT WORK AND FAILS SILENTLY** — stancl's `CentralConnection` trait makes `getConnectionName()` ignore every override, so the row lands on the default connection inside `RefreshDatabase`'s transaction and the failure surfaces later, elsewhere, as an FK violation. Committed tenant fixtures must use the query builder. **ONE CONSOLE TRANSITION PER TEST** — a second PATCH re-reads through the elevated connection and sees the ORIGINAL committed status, so it is validated against the wrong `from` and usually refused *silently*, because a refusal is also a redirect-back. Assert `assertSessionHasNoErrors()`, never a bare `assertRedirect()`. **THE SCREENSHOT DECISION IS ADR-0015 AND THE PRD NOTE IS CLOSED** — native `getDisplayMedia`, zero dependencies, with the file input rendered ALWAYS rather than as a failure fallback; do not re-open the html2canvas option (it renders the Leaflet picker and the signature canvas blank). **`--mds-color-border-subtle` DOES NOT EXIST** — `token-references.test.ts`, the suite this file once called a known flake, caught it. Use `--mds-color-border-default`. **A COMMITTED CROSS-CONNECTION FIXTURE MUST BE PURGED, AND `SuperAdminBypassTest`'s "do not clean up" NOTE DOES NOT TRANSFER.** It commits `users`; these suites commit **tenants**, and nine unrelated tests went red in CI on a green local tree because of it. `purgeCommittedFeedbackFixtures()` (tests/Pest.php) is registered via `$this->beforeApplicationDestroyed(...)` from `beforeEach` — RefreshDatabase registers its rollback the same way during `setUp`, i.e. earlier, and Laravel runs them in order, so the DELETE happens after the transaction is gone and cannot deadlock. Key the purge on MARKERS, never ids, so a test that dies mid-way is cleaned by the next one. **THE TENANT `/feedback` PAGE IS READ-ONLY ON PURPOSE**: Feature #11 gives the lifecycle to the platform team, and a tenant marking its own report Resolved would be resolving it in the operator's queue.

**I6 (do not re-open):** **THE `/` ROUTE IS ONE ROUTE ON PURPOSE, AND THE OBVIOUS "FIX" BREAKS A PINNED TEST.** Do not add `Route::domain(config('tenancy.central_domain'))` to `routes/web.php` and move the tenant root into `routes/tenant.php`. The load-order half of the old note was right — `web.php` registers ~70 slots ahead of anything in `tenant.php` (mapped from `TenancyServiceProvider` inside `app->booted()`), so a `Route::get('/')` there is dead code — but the constraint that would free it up also stops `/` matching **custom** hosts, at which point the tenant route answers them and `InitializeTenancyBySubdomain` throws `NotASubdomainException` → **302 to `app.url`**, where `CustomDomainRoutingTest:158-167` pins a **404**. A host the platform does not own must not learn that a path exists, and the redirect would additionally disclose the canonical hostname. It would also diverge `localhost` from `127.0.0.1` locally, since both sit in `central_domains` while only one is `central_domain`. `RequirePlatformHost` already draws exactly the right line, so `PlatformLandingController` branches on `PlatformHost::subdomainLabel()`/`tenantFor()` **inside** the existing route; `PlatformLandingTest`'s last case asserts "404, **and specifically not a redirect**" so the change fails loudly rather than quietly. **`$request->user()` IS SAFE THERE WITH NO TENANT GUC** and this looks wrong next to `OpenTenantRegistrationTest`, which records that a per-tenant *settings* question in that state fails closed and silently: identity is the exception, because `config/auth.php`'s `users` provider is **`rls_aware`** and resolves on `pgsql_auth` precisely so login/registration/reset work against the fail-closed join-shape policy. **Do not add `EstablishTenantDatabaseContext` to that route.** The closure became a controller because a route **closure makes `route:cache` throw**. **`Welcome.vue` STAYS SELF-LAID-OUT** — `app.ts` excludes it by name, DSR §3.0's two shells do not fit a guest hero (`AuthLayout` is ~400px), and it is logged as **exceptions-log #8**; it is the first and only consumer of `--mds-type-display` / `--mds-space-16` / `--mds-space-24`, which the DSR has reserved for this class of surface since G11. Its `registrationOpen` prop must stay: without it the front door offers a button `GateRegistration` 404s. **`DatabaseSeeder`'s `WithoutModelEvents` MUST NOT COME BACK** — it wraps the whole run and every `->call()`ed child in `Model::withoutEvents()`, killing `HasUuidv7::creating` (the uuid PK; `users.id` has no DB default) and `BelongsToTenant::creating` (`tenant_id`, without which strict RLS refuses the row). There are **zero Observer classes in this repository**, so it protected nothing and broke `migrate:fresh --seed` for months, unseen because **nothing had ever executed that file**; `DatabaseSeederSmokeTest` is now the thing that would catch it. **`DemoSeeder` MUST NEVER WRITE INTO `acme`** — `E2eSeederIdempotencyTest` pins that fixture's counts exactly, and coupling a convenience fixture to a merge-blocking gate is how an unrelated PR starts failing. **THE AUDIT TAIL'S BACK-DATING IS AN INSERT, NOT AN UPDATE, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE WHOLE PERMISSION** — `audits` is `append_only` (strict SELECT + strict INSERT, **no UPDATE/DELETE policy at all**), so `forceFill(...)->saveQuietly()` is structurally impossible there while supplying `created_at` on the INSERT is fine. **Do not add an `occurredAt` parameter to `AuditLogger`**; `E2eSeeder.php:1096-1099` forbids it and this did not need it. `redacted_fields` on those rows is **produced by `AuditRedactor`**, never authored — the same discipline `AnswerIndexProjector` enforces for the index, and for the same reason: a hand-written redaction list is a fiction the viewer then renders as fact. **THE REVIEWER'S GRANT SET DELIBERATELY EXCLUDES `Patient Intake`** — it carries ~420 of the ~520 rows, so granting it made the reviewer see 446 where an Owner sees 497, which is correct and completely invisible; excluding it makes it 104 vs 497. `reviewer` and `form_editor` lack `dashboard.org.view`, so **without seeded grants their inbox is empty and the guide fails on its first review step**. **`resolveOrCreateUser()`'s PER-RUN MEMO IS LOAD-BEARING** — `consultant@demo.test` is resolved twice in one `run()`, and under `RefreshDatabase` the second `pgsql_auth` lookup cannot see the first, uncommitted, insert; without the memo it 23505s **in tests only**, for a seeder that works perfectly from the CLI. **`$historyDays` IS ONLY SOUND BECAUSE EVERY DERIVED VALUE IS A FUNCTION OF THE ROW INDEX, NEVER THE ROW COUNT** — that is what makes a short spec a genuine prefix of a long one, and `it('derives every submission row from its index')` pins it directly; if a formula ever divides by the count, the cheap tests stop proving anything about what ships. **Two corrections to this file's own earlier claims, both verified:** `phpunit.xml` pins Pest to **`meridian_testing`**, so the suite CANNOT wipe the dev database — only Playwright can, because it serves from `.env`; and the settings surface carries eleven cards (Profile · Appearance · Notifications · Drafts · Access · Maintenance · Modules · Custom domains · Branding · Security · About), with **no bare `GET /admin` route** at all (the console's entry point is `/admin/tenants`). **`docs/TESTING-GUIDE.md` IS NOW A MERGE-BLOCKING DOC** in the same sense the data dictionary is: an increment that changes a surface the guide walks must update the guide in the same PR, and §17's "what is not built" table must lose the row it just built.

**I4 (do not re-open):** **THE FEED IS A JSON SIDECAR, AND THAT SUPERSEDES THIS FILE'S OWN EARLIER INSTRUCTION.** The I-map and the previous NEXT PROMPT both said "unread count as a **LAZY** Inertia prop"; that is now wrong, and the reason is measured rather than stylistic. An `Inertia::optional()` prop is refreshed by a partial reload, and **a partial reload RE-DISPATCHES THE CURRENT PAGE'S CONTROLLER** — Inertia filters what it SERIALIZES, not what it COMPUTES. `AuditLogPresenter::index()` and `SubmissionInboxPresenter::list()` both paginate eagerly, so a 60-second tick with `/audit-log` open would buy a full ledger paginate plus a `count(*)` and discard it, forever, in every open tab. `GET /notifications` → `{unread_count, items[15]}` instead, and **nothing about notifications enters `HandleInertiaRequests::share()`**. Do not "fix" this back into a prop. **Popover-only** — no `/notifications` page and no nav item; the bell IS the centre, and PRD #13 asks for "recent events". **Both writes answer 204 through `builderClient`**, a NAMED EXCEPTION to the "every web-route mutation is an Inertia visit" rule that `scopesClient`/`integrationsClient` state — that rule exists because `bootstrap/app.php` keys its domain-exception branch on the `api/v1/*` PATH, and neither of these routes raises one. The reason it MUST be a fetch: Inertia's request stream is `maxConcurrent: 1, interruptible: true`, so a `router.post()` fired in the same tick as the row's `<Link>` navigation is **silently aborted** — click-to-read is unimplementable as an Inertia visit without a wasted second round trip. **In-app row copy is built SERVER-side** in `NotificationCopy::inApp()`, and I3's docblock claiming the opposite is amended: `data` is a six-arm discriminated union only PHP authors, where an exhaustive `match` makes an eighth case a FATAL and a TS `default:` arm makes it a blank row nobody notices. TS owns the icon and the colour band and nothing else. **A ROW'S `url` IS REACHABILITY-RESOLVED, NOT MERELY SLASH-JOINED, AND THAT IS NOT DEFENSIVENESS.** `SubmissionPolicy::view()` is `submissions.view && (org-wide || collaborates on the form)` with **no respondent clause**, yet `submission_approved`/`submission_returned` are addressed to `respondent_user_id` — so a form editor whose grant is later revoked keeps a row pointing at Laravel's **bare 403 page, outside the Inertia shell, with no navigation back**. `/webhooks/{id}` additionally needs `feature:webhooks`, and all three target tables soft-delete. The presenter batch-loads the targets, runs the real `Gate`, and ships `url: null` otherwise, so the row renders as plain text. **Widening the policy is I9's call** — `docs/feature-backlog.md` §4 carries the row; do not do it as a drive-by. **No permission key and no `feature:` gate, both asserted by `NotificationRouteGuardsTest`** so neither can be tidied into symmetry with the four gated neighbours: a notification is addressed to ONE person by `user_id`, so authorization is `scopeForUser()`, and PRD #13 says notifications ship on **every** tier. **`Notification::scopeForUser()` IS THE AUTHORIZATION** on the list, on the unread count and on the mark-all UPDATE — strict RLS scopes to the TENANT only, so an unscoped mark-all silently clears every member's inbox, unaudited; `NotificationReadTest` pins it from both sides. The per-row write is gated on the BOUND INSTANCE by `NotificationPolicy::markRead`, the `SavedReportViewPolicy::owns()` case verbatim. **THE POLL STOPS AFTER 30 IDLE TICKS, AND THAT IS A SECURITY DECISION I8 DEPENDS ON** — every poll touches the session, so an eternal one keeps an abandoned tab authenticated forever and deletes `config/session.php`'s 120-minute idle expiry. It also tears the timer down entirely while the tab is hidden, and a failed poll NEVER blanks the bell (null means "ask again later", not "you have nothing"). **The E2E fixture keeps one row on the REVIEWER on purpose** — Playwright only logs in as the Owner, so a regression that dropped `scopeForUser()` would be invisible in an all-Owner fixture; the badge must read 4 while the table holds 7. **`MdsSwitch` is still I5's row** — the preferences card uses `MdsCheckbox`, and its two locked email controls are CHECKED + DISABLED + `aria-describedby` rather than switches wired to nothing (`honorsEmailPreference()` is false for `export_ready`/`webhook_failed`). **Three defects fixed that were not on the plan:** `GeneratePdfJob` records an `export_ready` row for EVERY terminal outcome, so a failed export said "Export ready" over a submission with no artifact (the email arm was always honest); the first spelling of that fix was `!== Ready`, which renders a MISSING outcome as a failure and contradicts its own sentence, caught by its own test; and `SubmissionInboxPresenter::pdfArtifact()`'s docblock had asserted "there is no notification bell, no polling" since before I3 merged.

**I3 (do not re-open):** **`review.requested` IS DELIBERATELY NOT A `DomainEventType` CASE, and adding one later would be a regression.** The I-map named it; there is no request-review verb in the product (`markUnderReview()` is a reviewer *claiming* a submission, the opposite), and that enum is ALSO the webhook + Slack **subscription** vocabulary — so a case with no emission site is a checkbox a tenant can tick that can never fire. `NotificationType::ReviewRequested` is a second fan-out of `submission.created` instead, targeted at reviewers, and `NotifyOnSubmissionCreated` SUBTRACTS the `submission_received` recipients from it so an Owner (who is in both role sets) gets ONE row per submission, not two. **The three cases I3 did add shipped with their six fan-out twins in the same commit** (`DispatchWebhooksFor*` + `DispatchConnectorsFor*`), for exactly the same reason — `WebhookFanOutTest` pins that a subscriber actually receives them. **Adding a `DomainEventType` case is a five-file act**, now written into the enum's own docblock: a migration recreating `webhook_deliveries_event_type_check` (else 23514 on the first delivery), a label — which since I3 lives ONCE on `DomainEventType::label()` rather than in the two presenters' byte-identical exhaustive `match`es — Slack copy + a deep link, a regenerated `openapi.json` (4 inline arrays; CI job 5 byte-diffs it), and the `eventTypes` count assertions, which are now `count(DomainEventType::cases())` rather than literals. **BOTH TABLES ARE `strict`, NOT `belongs_to_user`, AND THAT IS NOT STYLE** — `belongsToUserSql()` emits policies keyed only on `user_id` with NO tenant predicate, so a consultant in two tenants reads across both; `migration-lint` cannot catch it (it checks only that *some* `withTenantIsolation()` exists), so "mine" is the application predicate `scopeForUser()` and every read path must apply it. **`notifications` COLLIDES WITH LARAVEL'S OWN TABLE NAME** — `User` uses `Notifiable`, so `$user->unreadNotifications` now fails with `column notifications.notifiable_type does not exist`, which reads like a bug in this increment and is not; `'database'` is a forbidden channel repo-wide and `NotificationTableGuardTest` pins it. **`data` MAY NEVER HOLD A TOKEN OR A SIGNED URL** — under strict RLS every member of the tenant can read those rows and nothing redacts them (both tables are deliberately UNAUDITED, argued in audit-spec §1: a notification is a derivative of an already-audited act, and `read_at` churn would flood a ledger that is never pruned). **`MemberInvited` carries no accept URL and the invitation email did NOT move behind it** — the plaintext token exists only as a local in `invite()`, the URL must be built in-request under live tenant context, and the payload is webhook-visible, so an accept link there would be privilege escalation dressed as an integration; the event carries `{email, role, invited_by, expires_at}` and `email` is a **stated deviation** from webhook-design §3's default-exclude principle, recorded there rather than shipped silently. `returned_reason` is excluded from BOTH the webhook payload and the in-app row for the same principle. **`SubmissionReviewService::apply()` NO LONGER RETURNS `DB::transaction()` DIRECTLY** — an announcement inside the closure fires for a transition that then rolls back, and `DB::afterCommit` is verified never to fire under the suite's uncommitted outer transaction; `SubmissionReviewEventsTest` proves the ordering by reading `DB::transactionLevel()` from INSIDE the listener, which is the assertion `assertDispatched` cannot make. `markUnderReview`/`archive` announce NOTHING, deliberately, and the absence is asserted. **`ResourceGrantResolver` GAINED `primeFor()`, AND IT IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT AN OPTIMIZATION** — recipient resolution runs on a SYNCHRONOUS listener inside a public guest POST, so a per-candidate `holdsAny()` is one grant query per collaborator-scoped member in that request; the prime is one query and a bounded-query test (≤6 for 12 editors) is what stops a regression to per-user. It is a PRIME rather than a `granteesOfForm()` so the answer still comes from `decide()`, the one interpreter of `resource_grants`. Pass `holdsAny()` a **Form model, never an id** — the id branch re-anchors the form against the database on every call. **The recipient rule IS `SubmissionPolicy::view()`**, and `NotificationRecipientResolverTest` asserts agreement with `Gate::forUser()` itself rather than with a restated expectation: the bell must never announce a submission the inbox 403s on. Org-wide roles are DERIVED from `dashboard.org.view` in the permission catalog, never read off `RolePermissionSeeder::MATRIX` (app code reaching into a seeder for an authorization decision survives right up until someone edits the matrix). **`export_ready`/`webhook_failed` keep their purpose-built branded emails** and get an in-app row via `NotificationDispatcher::record()`, not `dispatch()` — `honorsEmailPreference()` is false for exactly those two, so their email is not preference-gated (both are operational and addressed to the one person who caused or owns the thing) and I4 must render those toggles as unavailable. **`EventNotification` is ONE class for the whole catalog** and needed BOTH registration sites (`QueuedMailContractTest`'s array AND `job-payload-lint`'s EXEMPT_JOBS) — two separate gates, and the linter test reads the script as a STRING, so the FQCN must match literally.

**I2 (do not re-open):** **`audits.auditable_id` IS KEYED ON THE OWNING TENANT OR USER WHENEVER THE TARGET'S PK IS NOT A UUID, AND THAT IS NOT DEFENSIVENESS.** `domains.id` is an `increments()` INTEGER (`create_domains_table.php:23`) while the column is `uuid NOT NULL` — a domain audit keyed on the domain row inserts `'3'` into a uuid column and **500s with `SQLSTATE 22P02` on the first live claim**, invisible to PHPStan, to the `audits_event_check` and to the migration linter. It is spec §1's role-grant device (`model_has_roles` has a composite PK and no surrogate id) applied a second time, it is written into §1 and data-dictionary §13 so nobody "fixes" it back, and **`AuditDomainSitesTest`'s first test is its only mechanical guard — mutation-verified**: reverting the key reddens with that exact error rather than a soft assertion. The hostname rides in `old_values`/`new_values.domain` because the key cannot carry it and `domains` rows are deletable, so a join would not always answer it either. **THE SECOND DOMAIN HAZARD IS THE ONE RLS CANNOT SEE.** `domains` is RLS-EXEMPT and `audits` is not, and the `audits` INSERT `WITH CHECK` compares `tenant_id` to the **AMBIENT GUC**, never to the row being described — so an operator path holding tenant B's domain under tenant A's context would **succeed** in filing B's event into A's ledger. `CustomDomainService::auditDomain()`'s `$tenantId !== $domain->tenant_id` check IS that isolation, exactly as the class docblock says of its `where('tenant_id', …)` filters. It also refuses when there is no context at all, and it **fails SILENT on purpose**: the sweep is a `$tries = 1` `MaintenanceJob`, so a throw there lands in `failed_jobs` on every tick forever — an audit gap is recoverable, a permanently-failing sweep is not. **`verify`/`activate`/`deactivate`/`sweep` ARE DELIBERATELY UNAUDITED and the tests pin the ABSENCE**, so a later reader cannot mistake a decision for an oversight: `verify()` is reached from HTTP *and* the console (a conditional audit would make the ledger depend on which caller won a race, and it records a check result the sweep rewrites every 15 minutes forever); `activate`/`deactivate` are artisan-only with **no actor**, so a row would carry `user_id = null` beside `is_system_action = false` — malformed by `AuditLogger`'s own contract. **`verification_token` STAYS OUT OF THE PAYLOAD AND OUT OF `AuditRedactor::SECRETS`** — the migration at `2026_08_04_000001…:49-51` already decided it is not a credential (it is published in public DNS at a name only the zone's controller can write); keeping a non-secret out of a payload is a different act from redacting it and must not drift into one. **`settings` ROWS ARE KEYED ON `tenants.id` PERMANENTLY, INCLUDING AFTER I5 CREATES THE `settings` TABLE** — pinned in spec §1 now precisely so I5 inherits the decision instead of making a silent one: settings are a per-tenant singleton, and keying new rows on a settings-row uuid would split one resource's history across two `auditable_id`s and make it unqueryable by `audits_tenant_auditable_idx`. Branding uses that same alias rather than a `branding` one (it renders inside `/settings` and gates on `tenant.settings.manage`; a second alias fragments one surface across two filter options), and **`brand_ramp` is never in a payload** — 2 KB of derived contrast tables that are a pure function of `primary_color`, in a table that is never pruned. **`submissions.remarks` IS REDACTED AND `returned_reason` IS NOT, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE DECISION:** remarks are internal free text with no audience discipline (a reviewer can paste respondent PII into them) and `audits` is never deleted, while the returned reason was authored FOR the respondent and already emailed to them — it is exactly the accountability the ledger exists to preserve. **THE §2.1 RENDERING RULE IS THE ONE MOST LIKELY TO BE "FIXED" INTO A DEFECT:** a redacted key already carries the placeholder on BOTH sides when it is written, so `AuditDiff` emits it verbatim on both and flags the key — it never reconstructs, nulls or hides it, and the UI shows a *Redacted* badge rather than printing `[REDACTED]` as if it were the value, above a notice that says the original was never recorded **including the "before" value**. **`is_system_action` IS DEAD DATA** — `AuditLogger` hard-codes it `false` and exposes no parameter, so it is false on 100% of rows; the page derives `is_system` from `user_id === null` instead, which is the condition that actually varies. The actor has **THREE** states, not two: `System` (null actor), a name, and **`Unknown user`** for a live non-null `user_id` whose row the join-shape `users` policy now hides because the membership was removed — rendering the last two identically would blame the platform for a person's act. **`event-variant.ts` IS A DELIBERATE DSR §3.8 DEVIATION**: it owns the badge COLOUR while `AuditEvent::label()` owns the LABEL (one string for the badge, the filter and the CSV). Do not fold it into `status-variant.ts` — `archived` is `warning` as a form STATE because an inert form needs attention, and an ordinary historical fact as an EVENT; amber on every such row spends the attention channel `exported` and `permission_changed` need, and `created`/`updated` in a map every screen reads would let a future status enum inherit an audit colour. **The export's self-referential row carries NO count** (the stream can be abandoned mid-body, so an intended count the client never received would be a false entry in the one table that exists not to contain those) and **the exported file contains its own export row** — accepted, pinned, and not to be tidied away by excluding `audit_log` rows, which would make the export lie about the ledger. **Two claims were WITHDRAWN after mutation testing refused to confirm them, and the withdrawals are in the code**: `applyLocal()`'s second argument does not keep the Actor column populated (the `users` policy resolves an active co-tenant through its membership branch with no user GUC), and `->with('user:id,name')` is an **N+1 guard no test enforces** because `data_get` lazy-loads. What IS verified: deleting `applyLocal()` empties the file at **HTTP 200**. **The URL is `/audit-log` and the page folder is `audit`** — the repo's first deliberate divergence (the URL matches the permission name and the PRD; the folder matches every sibling), so "fixing" either half breaks every `router.get` string on the page. **No column is `sortable`**: `MdsDataTable` sorts only the rows it was handed, so on a server-paginated ledger a sort header reorders 25 of 4,000 and lies (`webhooks/Show.vue` and `submissions/Inbox.vue` both carry that defect — noted, not copied, not fixed here). **The Filters `<h2>` is load-bearing**, not decoration: `PageHeader` renders the `<h1>` and `MdsEmptyState` an `<h3>`, so dropping it fails axe's heading-order **only in the empty state** — i.e. only on a database the seeder did not populate.

**I1 (do not re-open):** the QR is **server-rendered and that is not a preference** — `bacon/bacon-qr-code` v3.1.1 was ALREADY in `composer.lock` as a `laravel/fortify` dependency (Fortify server-renders the 2FA QR from it), and `XMLWriter` + `iconv` — its only requirements — were verified present in the container before the choice was made. Do not "modernize" it to a client-side npm library: that adds a production dependency under the `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` gate to redraw something that changes only when the author saves. It is a **direct `composer.json` require**, promoted with `composer update --lock` (a one-line content-hash diff, zero version movement) rather than `composer require` — leaving it transitive is how a `composer update` becomes an outage. It is delivered to an **`<img src>`, never `v-html`**: `TwoFactorSetup.vue:30` holds the only `v-html` in the entire `resources/` tree and is worth not making two of. **THE `frame-ancestors` ASYMMETRY IS THE DESIGN, AND "FIXING" IT BREAKS THE PRODUCT.** The public runtime states `frame-ancestors *` because PRD:187 *requires* third-party iframe embedding; the authenticated app states `frame-ancestors 'none'` via the new `AppSecurityHeaders`. **`AppSecurityHeaders` MUST NOT be mounted globally on `web`** — it is named on four groups explicitly (authenticated tenant app, invitations/branding, central platform pages, Fortify auth routes), because `web` also carries the guest runtime and a global mount would kill every embed in the product. **⚠️ THE DEFECT A TEST FOUND WAS MINE, AND IT WAS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE I PLANNED FOR.** `AppSecurityHeaders` **REPLACES** an inherited `frame-ancestors` rather than deferring to one. The first draft deferred — "a policy that already decided framing knows better" — which is false here: `PublicRuntimeSecurityHeaders` is *also* mounted on the authenticated manual-encode page (`routes/tenant.php`, for its OSM `img-src`), so deferring handed the guest runtime's `frame-ancestors *` to a **session-bearing page**, opening a clickjacking hole with the very change meant to close one. The sibling hazard is still live and is why the merge exists at all: if the middleware order ever reverses, `PublicRuntimeSecurityHeaders`' no-clobber guard trips and **the OpenStreetMap tile allowlist silently vanishes** — a blank map on one page with no error in any log. Both are pinned by `AppSecurityHeadersTest`'s last test, which asserts BOTH policies land on that one route. Do not add either middleware to `bootstrap/app.php`'s priority list. **Do NOT introduce `default-src`** — `PublicRuntimeSecurityHeadersTest`'s four negative assertions (`default-src`/`script-src`/`connect-src`/`font-src`) are a deliberate tripwire (piping-design §262); `frame-ancestors` was safe to state alone precisely because it has **no `default-src` fallback**, so it narrows nothing else. **`FormSlug::isTaken()` uses `withTrashed()` and that is a BUG FIX, not defensiveness:** `forms_tenant_id_public_slug_unique` has no `deleted_at` predicate, so a soft-deleted form squats its slug forever — a scope-respecting check calls it free and the write dies on a 23505. **The fix was mutation-verified**: reverting `withTrashed()` reddens the test with the exact wrong value. Note the two halves differ — `Rule::unique('forms', …)` queries the TABLE and already sees trashed rows correctly; the bug was only ever in the *suggestion* loop. **A slug change invalidates NOTHING** (asked and answered): share tokens are HMACs over (tenant, form, version) and resume links carry a token, not a slug, so a guest mid-form and a saved draft both survive a rename — only the old `/f/{slug}` dies, which is exactly what the modal warns about. Pinned in `FormShareTest`. **The guest toggle is `MdsCheckbox`, deliberately** — `MdsSwitch` is DSR §3.2's forward spec and belongs to **I5**; building it here would have collided with that row. **The Share panel's URL is a SERVER PROP, never `window.location`** — `TenantUrl::toPublic()`, because a custom domain serves the guest runtime only (ADR-0012 §D1) and a browser-derived host is wrong for exactly the Business tenants who paid for it. **The address preview is suppressed once it would restate the live link** — showing the same URL twice under two headings reads as two different addresses, the H24b1 contradiction class. **Per-form rate limiting / CAPTCHA is an UNBUILT PRD Feature #3 criterion**, not built here and not dropped: it is an explicit **I8** line item and a `docs/feature-backlog.md` row.

**H24b1 (do not re-open):** the chart scale is **derived**, and the derivation is in the tests, not in prose — `theme-overrides.test.ts` recomputes every ratio from `tokens/chart.json` and the CSS on each run, so darkening a series past its floor reddens the suite rather than the eye. **Do not "simplify" the two colour sets into one**: put the light column on the dark ground and four of five fail 1.4.11 (worst 1.56:1); the raw Okabe-Ito anchors fail white from the other end. **Do not add a `[data-accent]` rule for a chart token** — the pre-existing accent-scope guard tested only the five `--mds-color-*` families and would not have caught it, so a second assertion was added for `--mds-chart-*` specifically. **`--mds-chart-other/-grid/-axis` alias NEUTRAL primitives on purpose** and must not be given a dark re-point: the dark block already flips the ramp underneath them, and re-pointing them would freeze them against the flip. **Do not emit the sequential scale** until something consumes it. **NEVER interpolate a token name into `var()`** — `token-references.test.ts` regexes source and would hunt for a token literally named `--mds-chart-series-`; series colour is a static CSS class per index, which also keeps `stroke-dasharray` off presentation attributes. **The §D12 data table is pinned by VITEST and cannot be pinned by axe** — axe passes a chart that is a beautiful unreadable picture, so deleting a table assertion loses coverage no gate replaces. **The value domain always includes zero and the API does not offer to turn it off**; a degenerate domain (an all-zero month — the ordinary state of a quiet tenant) is widened rather than returning one tick, because every consumer divides by the span and the alternative is a silent `NaN` path. **No text lives inside the time-series SVG** — the canvas is stretched with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, so text would stretch too; axis labels are HTML on a CSS grid and the y-column's half-line-height bleed is what puts a tick label's centre on its gridline, not a nudge. **The bar chart's label is HTML beside a one-`<rect>` SVG** for the same class of reason: SVG text neither wraps nor ellipsises. **Past five series the extras are TABULATED, not dropped** — folding them into an *Other* bucket is the caller's call. **The form label is resolved in `DashboardMetricsService`, never in `AnalyticsMetricsService::breakdown()`** (that shape is the byte-diffed API response) and the lookup is **`withTrashed()`**, because an org-wide reader's visible set is rooted on `Form::withTrashed()` and a soft-deleted form otherwise renders as a bar with no name. **The two draft tiles do not share a sentence**: `conversion_rate` is null only at a zero denominator, `median_seconds` is *additionally* null when the denominator is positive and nothing converted — collapsing them put "0% of 6 saved drafts" beside "No drafts were explicitly saved" on one screen. **Standing lesson reinforced — a defect the gates cannot see is found by LOOKING.** Every gate was green when that contradiction was on screen; it was caught in a screenshot, then turned into a test that was run against the unfixed component first and genuinely reddened. **Second standing lesson — a recorded flake is a liability, not a fact of life.** `token-references` had timed out intermittently since H21b and H21d1 found a real dangling token behind one such timeout; this increment adds files to the tree it walks, so the 5 s default was replaced with an explicit 30 s budget rather than pushing a known-dangerous flake closer to the edge.

**H24a (do not re-open):** the shape is ADR-0011's, implemented. **The three post-approval corrections are load-bearing and were each verified against the tree before being believed** — the join-side gap in §D2, the `E2eSeeder` no-op, and the `date_trunc` timezone hazard. **TWO MUTATIONS CAME BACK GREEN AND BOTH INDICTED THE CODE, NOT THE SUITE**: `applyCountableJoin()` had ZERO callers (the aggregator roots on `submissions` and joins OUTWARD, which is strictly better and makes §D2 structural rather than remembered — the helper and its drifted docblock are gone), and the export's three §D13 lines were ceremony until the aggregation moved INSIDE the closure. A third mutation — the permissions team-id restore in the export closure — stays green, and that one indicts the TEST: every analytics route's `can:` gate warms Spatie's role memo on the same User instance first, so the unwarmed case cannot be constructed on any route that exists. It is kept anyway, because the alternative is depending on an invariant enforced in another file, and the docblock says so rather than pretending the line is covered. **§D13 NAMES applyLocal() BUT THE GUC IS ONLY HALF THE TORN-DOWN CONTEXT** — the permissions TEAM id is gone too and `AnalyticsFormSet` consults `$user->can()`, so an org-wide reader could silently export their granted-forms subset: a SMALLER number, in a file, with no error. **The index addendum stayed ONE by WIDENING H12a's capacity index in place** rather than adding a second; the net count is asserted, so a later increment cannot quietly append one. **The shape is pinned STRUCTURALLY, not by `EXPLAIN`** — a repo lesson reused rather than relearned (`ScopeNodeScaleTest:120-128`: on a small table the planner tie-breaks unpredictably, so a plan-shape assertion measures the cost model, not the property). **§D6's scope-node axis has THREE dispositions, not two**: forms on a deactivated node fold into *Unassigned*, because dropping them makes `sum(buckets) < total` on the same screen — the §D2 disagreement in miniature. **§D5's denominator is `source IN (guest, manual)`**, a forward-dated defect fixed before it lands: `SubmissionDraftService` is a SERVICE seam and every `createDraft()`/`updateDraft()` path stamps `last_saved_at`, so the moment H18/H19 ship, OCR-staged drafts would turn the median into encoder review latency with no visible change. **Suppression has THREE states, never two** — suppressed / unavailable / a number — because `percentile_cont` returns NULL on an empty set and a careless shape renders suppression as 0%, which is §D5's failure wearing a different hat. **Both `hidden` and `calculated` are Indexable**: the projector never consults `field_type` and `SubmissionFinalizer` merges `$result->computed` into the map before projecting, so classification follows what happens, not what feels like respondent data.

**H1e (do not re-open):** the doc is **`docs/adr/0011-analytics-substrate.md`** — an **ADR, not a numbered design doc** (ADRs share Doc #7, so `docs/PRD.md:13`'s index correctly still tops out at **#27**), and it is **0011, not 0010**: 0010 stays reserved for H1d, and the ADR-0009 number-reuse precedent does **not** transfer, because payments was *cancelled and never written* (a permanent hole) whereas H1d is *blocked* and still expected (a temporary one). **THERE IS NO FORM-ENGAGEMENT EVENT STREAM IN PHASE 3** and H-map row 259 + its hazard note are rewritten in place to say so — a future session reading the original row will design a `form_events` table, a Group-C beacon, a Dexie telemetry queue and a partitioning scheme that this ADR deliberately declined, because `PRD.md:203-206`'s three acceptance criteria need none of them and the alternative was a fourth unauthenticated ingress class writing the highest-volume table in the system onto one self-hosted box. Do not "restore" it: the deferral is reasoned, and its two costs (offline respondents emit no events; "step 3" is not the same node for two respondents under a relevance-filtered graph, so branching funnel semantics is its own unsolved problem) are recorded for Phase 4. **THE DENOMINATOR ARGUMENT IS THE POINT OF §D5, NOT A CAVEAT.** A respondent who abandons leaves **no server row at all**, and the explicitly-saved drafts that do exist are hard-deleted nightly — so a completion funnel built on `count(status='draft')` returns **100% on a default-configured form and rises every night as the reaper deletes its own denominator**. `PRD.md:198` is amended in-repo; do not un-amend it, and do not ship an unqualified "completion rate" or "average completion time". `submitted_at − created_at` is ~0 for a direct submit (same transaction) and `finalized_at` is a **reviewer** timestamp, not a respondent one. **ANSWER-VALUE AGGREGATION IS OPT-IN AND PROSPECTIVE-ONLY.** `submission_answer_index` projects only `is_queryable` top-level scalars, only at finalize, and is **never backfilled** — flipping the flag indexes future submissions only *and* is a breaking-but-permitted schema change needing a new version. That is why the Phase-3 promise is submission **metadata**; do not widen it to "aggregate any question" without shipping the backfill job the ADR defers. Scalar *includes* `likert_scale` (a single chosen value) — do not write "likert is not indexable"; it is the two **grid** types that are not. **CHARTING IS IN-HOUSE SVG AND THE ARGUMENT IS NOT AESTHETIC:** `build-tokens.mjs` emits `var()` **references**, not resolved colours, and `useTheme` flips `data-theme-mode`/`data-accent` live, so a canvas library cannot resolve a token at paint time and goes stale on a theme flip. Five series is **derived** from the light+dark 3:1 luminance band, not chosen — which is also the proof that the dark re-point is mandatory. **No contrast ratios are printed in the ADR by design**; H24b measures and records them in DSR §4.1, because an ADR that prints ratios nobody computed is worse than one that prints none. **The surface is HIDDEN, not locked-with-upsell**, because Business is `is_active = false` — an upgrade CTA to an unpurchasable plan is a dead end, and the same fact is why H24b's e2e fixture must seed a Business-entitled tenant or the axe gate stays green over a page it never loaded. Finally: **`read:analytics` is a NEW ability but coins NO new RBAC permission** — `dashboard.org.view`/`dashboard.form.view` already encode exactly the org-wide-vs-own-forms split an analytics read needs. Do not fold the ability into `read:submissions` "to avoid another ability": that retroactively arms every already-minted token, which is the failure `ApiAbilities` documents twice in prose.

**H21d2 (do not re-open):** there is **ONE classifier**, `condition-model.ts`'s `toCondition(Node) → Condition | null`, and `condition-describer.ts` renders its sentence FROM the `Condition` it returns — so `say()` is TOTAL. Do not re-fuse a second classification into the describer "for prose": that is exactly how the rail and the editor would come to disagree about which expressions are safe to touch, which is the failure Doc #27 amendment D2 spent an increment avoiding. **The model must stay exactly as expressive as the describable set**, quirks included — a reversed comparison (`18 < ${age}`), a field-vs-field one (`${end} > ${start}`), a literal-vs-literal one (`1 = 1`). Do not "tidy" it into subject-is-always-a-field: that would silently narrow the RAIL's prose, and it is why both sides of a comparison are the same control rather than a subject and a value. `condition-describer.test.ts` passing byte-unchanged is the guarantee that the classification moved without its boundary moving. **THE PRINTER CHECKS ITSELF.** `serialize()` re-parses its own output and returns text only if it classifies back to a structurally equal `Condition`. Do not replace that with byte/token/depth constants — it is what turns three engine budgets into one refusal, and it is the only thing standing between a bad print and a draft that saves cleanly then fails at publish naming an expression the author never typed (**nothing validates `relevant_expression` on write**; `UpdateFieldRequest` is shape-only by decision). **The three refusals are real limits of grammar v2.0, not policy** — the lexer has NO escape sequences (so a value with both `'` and `"` is inexpressible), `lexNumber` has no exponent form, and `${…}` is ASCII `[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*`. **NO NEW WRITE ENDPOINT, and do not add one.** The editor writes `target.relevant_expression = text; store.touch(uid, kind)` into the existing PATCH; that is what inherits `builderClient`'s typed 409 rather than re-implementing it, and `FormBuilderController::respond()` stays the single place a 409 is minted. **`ConfigPanel` is REPLACED, not coexisting** — one stored value, one editor on screen, mode DERIVED from the value and never stored. **"Read-only" constrains the rows, not the author**: an opaque condition keeps a fully editable textarea, and the guarantee is that nothing parses, re-prints or touches it. That guarantee is mechanical — emit only from a user mutation, never from a watcher, never on mount, and never a value equal to the stored one. Do not add a canonicalize-on-open convenience; two shapes in the never-rewritten test (`${age}   >   18` and `(${age} > 18)`) exist to catch precisely that. **A half-built row is held, not written** (`isComplete()` gates the write) — writing the complete subtree and dropping the rest would be a silent partial save. **Standing lesson reinforced for the third increment running — a mutation that reddens NOTHING indicts the SUITE.** Deleting the printer's deep-equality check was green across 106 tests because the case that looked like it covered that arm is caught one branch earlier by the re-parse throwing. **Standing lesson reinforced twice now — re-run `token-references` in isolation.** It hid a real dangling token in H21d1 and again in H21d2.

**H21d1 (do not re-open):** the canvas is a **second view of the builder's centre pane**, not a page — one `useBuilderStore`, both views kept mounted, and `store.select` drives the existing `ConfigPanel`. Do not "extract" it into its own route: an author reads the map and edits in place, and a second page needs a second copy of the draft. **THE RAIL IS CLIENT-DERIVED AND LIVE; THE GRAPH ANALYSIS IS SERVER-DERIVED AND FLUSH-SCOPED**, and the split is the decision rather than an accident. Node order, each raw predicate, its reading, its `${key}` chips and its syntax errors come from the store and the TS `ExpressionParser` between keystrokes; the three §6 notices come from `GET /forms/{form}/graph`. **Do NOT port `StepGraphInspector` to TypeScript** — that was weighed and rejected as a sixth R3-style mirror pair with no corpus standing over it, and the one-save lag it costs is **shown** on screen (a "rechecking" line; a failed check that says so) precisely so nobody "fixes" the lag by building the mirror. The refetch is gated on `store.whenIdle()` and keyed on a **logic fingerprint**, not a deep watch — a label edit cannot create or clear a cycle. **THE DESCRIBER REFUSES TO PARAPHRASE, BY DESIGN.** One operand it cannot express makes the whole reading opaque; there are no partial readings, and grouping is preserved rather than flattened. Do not "improve" it into describing more shapes without deciding, per shape, that the sentence is *complete* — a confident wrong sentence about a form that decides who is asked what is the failure §8 calls the disqualifier. **`status: 'opaque'` IS H21d2's "non-representable" predicate**; reuse it rather than writing a second classifier. **Nothing here PRINTS an expression** — the AST→text printer is still H21d2's to build. **The rail draws AUTHORED order, not the taken path**: it is deliberately not built on `StepProjection`/`visibleSteps`, because there are no answers while an author is editing; a simulator is the backlog's residue. **The `${key}` chips are a display affordance, not an authority** — the publish gate still decides whether a reference resolves. **`emptyAtOpen()`'s `ExpressionException` catch is load-bearing**, not defensive: it wraps the whole evaluation because both settle loops can throw, and it exists because this is the first caller to run the inspector against an unvalidated draft. **New standing lesson — a known flake can be hiding a real failure, so re-run it in isolation before waving it through.** `token-references` fails as a timeout in the full Vitest run and has been recorded as a flake since H21b; in H21d1 the isolated re-run reported a genuinely undefined `--mds-type-body-font-size`, which without a fallback invalidates the whole declaration and makes the element scale by inheritance under `[data-font-size]`. **Second standing lesson — check new copy against the DARK palette specifically.** `--mds-color-danger-text` is `danger-300` in dark and fails contrast on `bg-surface` at 13 px; the `status-*-fg` family is the on-surface pair. The dark scan caught it, not the light one.

**H21c (do not re-open):** the encode page **MOUNTS `createFormRuntime()`** — the guest SPA's own store — so client-side parity is ONE implementation, not two that agree. Do not "decouple" it into a shared `projectSteps()` helper plus a local cursor: that was weighed and rejected, because Encode.vue would then re-implement the watcher, the rescue and `goToStep` — a second copy of everything H21b just built and pinned. The store was importable as it stood (`vue`, `../engine`, `../lib/schema-mapping`, `../lib/prefill`, `../lib/uuid`, `../lib/types`; no api-client, no Dexie, no DOM), and both tenant-app hazards were already handled — `randomUuid()`/`uuidv7()` are secure-context-safe and `buildPrefill(schema, '')` is a no-op for `url` sources, which is the correct reading of H7 here because **a keyer is the SOURCE of such a value, not a recipient**. **THE "REFERENCE FIELDS" BLOCK SITS OUTSIDE THE STEP LIST BY DECISION.** A section holding nothing but a `url`-hidden gate can never be a step (`RENDERS_NOTHING` drops `hidden`, and predicate 2 is a static type test), so those rows render in a labelled keyer-only block; a hidden field ALONGSIDE ordinary questions still renders inline where its author put it. The alternative — an audience axis on `StepProjection` and the TS store — was rejected because byte-identical step lists are what make §9's parity assertion a clean equality instead of an equality-modulo-hidden-only-sections. **THE PARITY SUITE ASSERTS THE INPUT** (amendment C4): with both channels on one function, running two projections and comparing them is a tautology in a cross-channel costume, so `EncodeStepParityTest` asserts that `version.schema` is byte-identical to `PublicFormPresenter`'s and that the taken path rebuilt FROM THE SHIPPED SNAPSHOT reproduces each fixture case. Do not "strengthen" it into a both-sides comparison. **`blocks` STAYS**, and its PHP↔TS normalizer duplication is deferred by name in Doc #27 §10 with a specific revisit trigger — the first time the two are found to disagree about a control's config. **THE CLOCK IS SERVER-STAMPED on this channel** (`version.now` → `RuntimeOptions.now`), unlike the guest's `isoClock(new Date())`, so the server-rendered `steps` and the client's first `visibleSteps` cannot disagree across midnight or a device skew. **NEXT NEVER BLOCKS** — `attemptNext()` is the guest's gate and would be a new refusal on a channel whose F4b contract is "the pipeline is the sole authority"; that is *why* Submit raises a FORM-WIDE summary whose jump links change step before they focus, and why the POST body is the full answer map rather than `effectiveAnswers` (which is what keeps the prune report meaningful as a divergence alarm). `preserveState` is a predicate, not a constant: a 422 must keep what was typed, a success must not. **`SubmissionPdfPresenter` IS NOT REFACTORED ONTO `StepProjection`** despite that class's docblock saying H21c "collapses them" — the PDF's "No entries." notice and em-dash blanks are distinctions the step model cannot express, so the honest collapse is the cross-check §9 asks for in words. **`min_instances` starter rows are still seeded here**, unlike the guest, which opens a repeat group empty so an empty guest load stays axe-clean. **New standing lesson — RUN A REGRESSION TEST AGAINST THE UNFIXED CODE BEFORE BELIEVING IT.** The first test written for the repeat-mask defect passed against the broken version: with one surviving row and one pruned row, the two implementations name different rows and render the SAME string. A test written to prove a fix is exactly as vulnerable to the more-than-one-path trap as a test written to specify a feature, and the check costs one `git show HEAD:<file> >` and one run. **Second standing lesson — a green mutation can indict an ACCIDENT rather than a test.** `EncodeFormPresenter::OMITTED` ⊂ `PdfFieldRole::Omitted` held by coincidence of two lists answering different questions, enforced nowhere; the mutation that exploited it reddened nothing, and the fix was to pin the relation rather than to add a test for the behaviour. Also: an assertion that reads a model relation by the wrong name yields `[]`, not an error, and `[]` makes every relevance gate read ABSENT — assert the fixture is non-empty before comparing anything derived from it.

**H21b (do not re-open):** the scope was the **whole of Doc #27 §9's H21b obligation**, not just the five live defects — §4.2, §5.2, §5.3 and §4.4's step notice ship here, so **H21c inherits nothing from H1f**. **The empty graph is a TERMINAL STATE**: the progress nav is suppressed entirely (never "Step 1 of 0"), `isLastStep` is `length > 0 && index === length - 1`, and a separate **`isTerminal`** computed carries the view — do not "simplify" it away, because with the guard in place `isLastStep` is correctly false at zero steps and the template would render Next instead of the single labelled Submit. **ONE `watch(visibleSteps, (next, prev))` replaces the membership-boolean watcher** and does seed-or-self-heal · rescue-by-identity · visited set · `StepChange`; the old boolean source was the bug (false at t=0, still false when steps appear, so it never fired). **§4.2's walk goes OUTWARD — backwards THEN forwards — and both arms are load-bearing**; the forward arm was pinned by nothing until mutation testing caught it (amendment **B2**, which corrects the doc's own wording). `nearestSurvivor()` returns **null** on failure ON PURPOSE so each caller keeps its own fallback — the rescue watcher takes the first step, `goToStep()` falls through to §5.3's first-incomplete — and folding either in makes the other unreachable. **"Done" comes from the store's VISITED SET**, never `index < currentIndex` (a step can become relevant behind the respondent). **§5.5: Submit reports FORM-WIDE errors, a blocked Next stays STEP-scoped**, and `SummaryBanner` got a `beforeJump` prop because a jump link cannot resolve a field on an unmounted step. **The replay guard reads `client.fetchSchema()` after `remint()`, memoized PER PASS**, sits BEFORE the media leg (the attachment controller carries the identical check against the same fresh token), and leaves `row.checksum` unread — the server compares the version id and matching it exactly is the point. **Resumed-draft rows park by decision, not oversight.** **The respondent-facing "% complete" is GONE** and its absence is asserted;
